### Bounded group holdings refresh for sell sessions, 11 October 2026

Selected sell-session wallets now refresh holdings together through an authenticated project-bound endpoint, addressing the conflict between a 60-second sizing window and individually gated wallet refreshes. Failed automatic reads retain the prior markers and display an error/manual reload notice.

# Product goal: make Bundle like Proxima

### Visible project wallet inventory and post-trade locks, 11 October 2026

Separated the project trade-wallet inventory from the bundle-session disclosure. Its own native Project trade wallets section starts open, keeping searchable saved wallet rows and per-wallet refresh/buy/sell handoffs reachable beside the chart. Bundle-session controls retain their mounted disclosure and existing state. The wide wallet table uses its existing horizontal scroll container, including on narrow trading panes; a more compact position layout remains desirable.

Project/mint-matching finalized receipt events flag the affected wallet's token inventory stale. Old observations remain visible as history; row-level sell percentage opening and already-open percentage reviews are locked for that wallet. A matching explicit wallet token-provider refresh followed by validated inventory read clears its flag only if no further receipt event arrived during the request. Reloading saved inventory alone leaves the stale flag in place. Other wallets remain usable. This is same-page freshness coordination, not persistent monitoring or authorization to sign.

Verification: five targeted wallet-rendering/event tests passed; TypeScript, focused lint/formatting and the five-stage build passed. Chrome synthetic-transport QA verified the inventory is initially expanded, a synthetic finalized receipt adds its freshness notice, the affected wallet's sell percentages are disabled, and the unaffected wallet's percentages remain enabled. Desktop/mobile screenshots bundle-open-wallet-inventory-qa.png and bundle-open-wallet-inventory-mobile-qa.png were inspected. Provider-refresh clearing and in-flight event generation protection were reviewed in code, not exercised against a live provider. Owned QA tab/server closed and viewport reset. No live provider request, funded transaction, signature, migration or publication occurred. Full Proxima parity remains active.

### Retained history during candle updates, 11 October 2026

Added a follow mode to the authenticated candle API and connected the foreground updater to it. Each fresh provider page replaces its entire time range while preserving actual candles before its earliest timestamp, retaining the newest 500 maximum. This preserves loaded older history during live updates without retaining superseded values or filling provider gaps. Context, observation freshness and newest-candle regressions are rejected; an empty followed page fails while preserving prior saved data. Latest-page freshness is recorded separately and older-exhausted state survives only when the oldest retained boundary is unchanged. Explicit manual latest refresh retains its existing history-replacement behavior. UI copy now distinguishes that reset from followed updates.

Verification: eleven targeted tests passed across candle parsing/retention, the foreground controller and a new actual D1 candle endpoint suite. They cover refreshed OHLC values, retaining earlier timestamps, replacing gaps inside the refreshed range, the 500 limit, stale/empty/foreign-page rejection, persistence and reload, owner rejection before provider access, pacing and failure retention. TypeScript, focused lint/formatting and all five build stages passed. Browser polling controls were verified in the preceding increment; this increment's persistence/merge behavior was verified through actual isolated D1 tests rather than the synthetic browser transport. No migration, live provider request, funded transaction, signature or publication occurred. Full Proxima parity remains active.

### Opt-in foreground candle updates, 11 October 2026

Added explicit Start/Stop candle updates to the production selected-pool chart. It uses the existing foreground controller to read the authenticated candle endpoint immediately and repeat 30 seconds after completion without overlapping reads. Hidden/offline tabs pause; failures require explicit restart. Pool, interval, project revision/archive and market-feed changes destroy the old controller, abort its request and derive inactive state for the new context. Manual refresh/older-page/interval controls are locked while running; chart inspection remains available. Reads have a 20-second timeout, preserve prior candles on failure and retain existing project/mint response checks. The sampled market feed must be stopped first. Latest-page refresh replaces loaded older history, clearly disclosed before start. This is foreground polling, not a tick stream or background scheduler.

Verification: eleven tests passed across the foreground controller, candle windows and provider parser. Added session-local wallet, group, direction and exact minimum-token-amount filters to the production supply alert inbox. ## Artwork upload progress, 9 October 2026

Classification: progress. Added persistent authenticated PNG/JPEG upload, token preview, hosted-URL replacement and removing an image reference from a draft. Stored references are relative, so they do not contain the development origin. Uploads are private per account, deduplicated by content within each account and retained when a project is archived. They are not publicly published token metadata.

Migration 0009 adds project_artwork. Streaming reads stop above 512 KB. Header and dimension checks limit supported images to 4096px; the browser also decodes the selected image before upload. SVG and other MIME types are rejected. Storage admits at most 100 images and 25 MB per owner atomically, while existing content can be reused at the cap. Other owners cannot retrieve an image, and image responses use private/no-store, nosniff and same-origin resource headers. Project-keyed forms abort pending clone/upload work when switching saved projects.

Verification: all 152 tests across 37 files passed, including actual D1 byte persistence, deduplication, owner isolation, quota enforcement, input bounds and safe reference validation. ## Project event history progress, 9 October 2026

Classification: progress. Project creation, saving, archive and restore now produce separate owner-scoped events. Historical names remain frozen, and save events identify changed fields. Activity links open the latest saved project. Existing projects remain available, but changes before this migration are not reconstructed.

Migration 0010 adds project_events and a mutation nonce on workspace_projects. Each project write and its event run in one D1 batch. Failed writes, stale revisions and cap conflicts emit no event; an event-storage failure rolls back the project mutation. The nonce prevents a losing concurrent write from recording a false event even when clocks return the same timestamp. History uses descending ID cursors, with 50 records per page and separate loading of older project events.

## Wallet folder workspace progress, 9 October 2026

Classification: progress. Live signed-in reference inspection confirmed a separate My Wallets folder surface with folder counts, archives, selection, management and export. Implemented an independent external-address roster in My wallets, separate from the public tracker. It supports six network profiles, folder creation/rename/archive/restore, bulk address entry, editable labels and Recipient/Deployer/Funder roles, selected bulk moves/archive/restore, search and public CSV export. These roles are explicit local launch-planning choices; the observed folder page did not establish Proxima role semantics. Adding an address does not establish ownership or create a signer.

Migration 0011_huge_donald_blake.sql stores owner-scoped wallet_folders and workspace_wallets. Integer revisions reject stale writes. Folder eligibility, whole-batch admission and all selected revisions are evaluated inside each mutation statement. Moving a set changes all selected wallets or none; archived destinations and cross-owner folders are rejected. Folder archive retains addresses and role labels. Restoring individually archived wallets requires an active containing folder. EVM addresses are checksum-validated and canonicalized before duplicate detection; Solana addresses must decode to 32 bytes. Account limits are 100 retained folders and 500 retained addresses, with 100 addresses per mutation. Only the existing local preview database received the migration.

## Native wallet balance progress, 9 October 2026

Classification: progress. Added authenticated native-balance refresh for all six roster networks, retained per-address observations, coherent current-view/selection totals and balance timestamps in public CSV export. Refresh supports up to 100 saved addresses in active folders. Unknown observations remain explicitly unobserved. Failed reads retain previous observations and display an error. Combined totals require every selected/view address to share one snapshot ID; independent snapshots are never presented as one total. Token holdings, USD valuation, P&L, ownership verification and live execution remain separate incomplete requirements.

Solana verifies the mainnet genesis hash, then requests ordered getMultipleAccounts with confirmed commitment and no account data bytes, providing one context slot. Original JSON integer tokens are preserved before Number rounding, including u64 lamports; unsupported integer precision fails closed. EVM readers verify chain ID, admit a recent block header and pin eth_getBalance calls to its canonical block hash with EIP-1898. Arc uses 18-decimal native USDC. RPC endpoints are fixed; account-specific configuration does not leak provider URLs or credentials. Calls share a 25-second deadline, EVM reads use eight workers, redirects are rejected and response bodies are limited to 512 KB.

Migration 0012_messy_zzzax.sql adds per-owner snapshots and a 15-second refresh admission gate. Snapshot writes are atomic across the requested set, recheck roster revisions and folder eligibility after RPC, and reject an observation older than an already retained block. A changed/archived roster or storage failure cannot partly replace a snapshot. Only the existing local preview database received this migration.

Verification: all 172 tests across 39 files passed. Ten new provider/precision tests cover all five EVM profiles, lossless Solana amounts above Number's safe integer limit, hash pinning, incomplete responses, network mismatches, stale headers, integer bounds, oversized/redirected responses and mixed-snapshot totals. Three new actual-D1 tests cover persistence, owner isolation, admission gating, provider failure retention, older-block rejection, folder changes during reads and atomic rollback. TypeScript, focused lint, production build and whitespace checks passed. The final copy cleanup received another clean production build, and the test-body cleanup passed all ten focused provider tests.

Live RPC probes verified chain IDs and canonical-hash balance reads on Robinhood, BNB, Arc, Ethereum and Base. Chrome exercised the complete authenticated Solana and Arc flows, including real RPC results and full reload readback. The public Arc QA address returned 0.050626 USDC at block 25107359; this is an account balance observation, not our funds or a profitability claim. ## External EVM wallet ownership progress, 9 October 2026

Classification: progress. My wallets now discovers EIP-6963 browser providers, retains a legacy browser-wallet fallback, connects an account on the selected Robinhood, BNB, Arc, Ethereum or Base network, and prefills the existing folder-entry form. An active saved account can request a five-minute ownership message and personal_sign approval. The roster displays a historical Signature verified timestamp after server verification. Private keys remain in the external wallet. No transaction methods, automatic network switches or mainnet execution were added. Contract-wallet signature validation is not supported yet.

Migration 0013_fresh_sandman.sql adds owner-scoped wallet_ownership_challenges and wallet_ownership_proofs. The signed message binds site origin, address, chain ID, a unique challenge and issuance/expiry. Challenges are capped atomically at 50 per owner and five per address; expired records are cleaned during challenge creation. Real ECDSA address recovery verifies the exact stored text. Consumption and proof storage use one D1 batch and a unique claim nonce, preventing replays and same-clock races. Eligibility is checked again inside consumption, and a failed proof insert rolls back the challenge. Only the local preview database received this migration. Stored proofs are historical observations, not continuing authorization to spend.

Provider requests are limited to eth_chainId, eth_requestAccounts, eth_accounts and personal_sign. Account and network checks run before and after signing. Account/network/disconnect events invalidate the local connection and pending requests; unmount and cancellation prevent later permission/signing steps. Discovery listeners and provider subscriptions clean up on unmount. Self-reported provider names render as text; wallet icons and rdns claims are not trusted or fetched.

Verification: all 188 tests across 41 files passed, followed by a clean TypeScript check and production build. Focused lint and whitespace checks passed. Chrome discovered MetaMask and Phantom and verified wallet selection and the rendered desktop connector. The existing wallet approvals were not submitted; a real extension approval and the complete connected-account-to-folder-to-signature UI flow remain unverified.

## Solana token holdings reader and retained snapshots, 9 October 2026

Classification: progress, with live provider integration incomplete. Added a per-wallet token holdings panel to the existing external-address roster. It queries both SPL Token and Token-2022 account programs, retains exact integer units and derived decimal balances, groups multiple accounts by mint/program, shows account addresses and frozen/extension status, supports mint search and zero-balance filtering, and labels observation slots and provider host. Token-2022 restrictions, prices and spendable balances are not inferred. Other networks retain native balances; token holdings discovery there is still missing.

The reader requires the Solana mainnet genesis, obtains a confirmed baseline slot, supplies minContextSlot to both enumerations and rejects responses below the baseline or more than 150 slots ahead. Each program's slot is retained; the combined inventory is an observation interval rather than a claim of one atomic slot. It verifies public key shapes, returned owner/program, account type and state, exact u64 token amounts, 0-255 decimals, consistent decimals per mint and duplicate account identities. It caps responses at 512 KiB and 500 accounts per program, uses a 25-second deadline and rejects the entire refresh if either enumeration is missing, malformed or fails. Larger inventories fail explicitly rather than appearing silently truncated.

Owner-scoped GET returns retained observations for saved wallets, including archived wallets. POST accepts only the owner's saved Solana wallets in active folders, shares the existing 15-second owner refresh gate with native balances, and conditionally writes one snapshot only if the wallet revision and active-folder eligibility still match. An older or overlapping observation interval cannot replace a newer retained snapshot. Provider and storage errors preserve earlier data. Generated migration 0015_smiling_bloodstorm was applied only to local D1. Browser requests cancel on panel changes/unmount and errors leave retained data visible. The panel heading receives focus when opened.

Verification: 217 tests across 44 files passed, plus TypeScript, focused lint, production build and whitespace checks. Eight reader tests cover exact arithmetic and aggregation, zero decimals, frozen Token-2022 accounts, complete empty versus incomplete enumeration, genesis/slot mismatches, unrelated owner/program, duplicates, conflicting decimals, overflow and account caps. Five actual-D1 holdings tests cover owner isolation, saved reads, exact grouped values and account status, rate limits, archived reads, wrong-chain/origin/content-type rejection, failed-provider retention, overlap rejection and changes to wallet/folder state while requests run.

Live verification is incomplete. Chrome saved an official Solana documentation example address A1TMhSGzQxMr1TboBKtgixKz1sS6REASMxPo1qsyTSJd in QA token holdings, opened the panel and exercised refresh failure. PublicNode's public endpoint answered scalar reads but blocked indexed token enumeration with HTTP 403 and a personal-token requirement. The official api.mainnet.solana.com endpoint successfully returned complete empty inventories from a direct Node read at slots 455018552 and 455018553, but the app's worker runtime received HTTP 403 even at getGenesisHash. A bounded OnFinality public-provider check returned HTTP 429; no OnFinality default or fallback remains. The final reader uses the app's existing getSolanaIndexerRpcEndpoint and existing SOLANA_INDEXER_RPC_URL configuration. Safe server diagnostics record only the failing method, provider hostname and HTTP status, not credential-bearing URLs or raw provider errors. No failed response was saved as an empty inventory. A usable indexer endpoint and successful worker-to-provider-to-D1-to-browser reload remain required.

Rendered proof: bundle-wallet-holdings.png and bundle-wallet-holdings-mobile.png show the actual unavailable-provider state. At a 390px viewport the document measured 375px and the panel 341px, with no horizontal page overflow. The public example wallet/folder was archived recoverably and temporary viewport sizing reset. No ownership claim, signing request or transaction was made. Full product parity remains active and incomplete.

Protocol references: https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/gettokenaccountsbyowner and https://solana.com/docs/tokens/basics/freeze-account . ## Quote-denominated holders buy inputs, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. Replaced the universal holders-percentage field with method-specific supply and quote inputs. Unscheduled Sequenced uses supply percentages; Quick, Stagger, Manual and scheduled planning use an amount denominated in the selected quote token. Unscheduled Stoxes automatic modes retain the captured supply-input exception. Manual labels the amount as holders funding. Developer percentages remain separate. Mode changes retain both independent values. Explicit quote, currency, network or venue changes clear the old quote amount unless the edit explicitly supplies a fresh amount. No percentage-to-currency conversion occurs.

Optional plan.holdersBuyQuote is a canonical decimal string. The structural parser allows non-negative plain decimals with a local storage bound of 30 whole digits and 18 fractional places, rejects numbers/exponents/signs/whitespace and trims insignificant fractional zeroes without floating-point arithmetic. New or changed plans validate the exact selected quote token's captured decimals. Ambiguous symbols require exact token selection. Missing quote amounts remain valid incomplete drafts and are displayed as missing; historical supply percentages are never treated as native amounts. The combined 100% structural allocation check now applies only to supply-input modes. Developer venue bounds apply independently across methods, and unsupported developer selections clear only on explicit selection changes.

Owner project JSON persistence retains the exact amount. The legacy launch-plans API reads/writes a nullable holders_buy_quote TEXT column, generated in migration 0017_loving_mesmero.sql. Fresh-schema SQL was updated, and the migration was applied successfully only to local D1 after the production build. New or changed plans in both APIs validate quote precision; rejected updates retain the old values and produce no project history event. Legacy amounts remain readable and unchanged archival remains supported.

The estimate panel displays supply allocations or the entered quote amount with its currency. It no longer adds developer percentages to dormant holders percentages in quote modes. Illustrative costs remain unavailable for quote-input modes, including Manual and scheduled scenarios. The entered amount excludes conversion, fees and wallet reserves; it is not a funding quote, execution estimate or proof that selected wallets are funded. No execution adapter, per-holder distribution, live quote or automated funding was added.

Scheduled Stoxes shows a discrepancy between the visible field condition and helper semantics; local scheduled display follows the captured form condition, and execution sizing for that combination remains unresolved. Advanced Meteora alpha-vault per-holder escrow semantics, continuous knot launches, imported-token execution and dynamic platform overrides remain outside this implementation.

## Saved holder distribution previews, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. Launch settings now supports an optional saved holder distribution with equal, deviation-weighted and one-wallet modes, explicit reshuffle and removal. The captured default is 50%. Random deviations through 100% use a range around the average; values above 100% use power-law weights. A retained seed preserves the preview across saves and reloads. Unassigned holder slots remain planning slots, not generated wallets. Selected same-chain Recipient references map to slots in their retained order; this address mapping was reviewed in code but was not exercised with selected addresses in this browser QA.

Quote allocations use the selected token's captured decimal precision and BigInt atomic amounts. Integer largest-remainder allocation conserves the exact entered total, including tiny amounts and zero-weight rows. Equal-split ties assign remainders to earlier slots. Supply-mode rows are planning percentages with at least twelve fractional places, not mint token units. The stable PRNG, normalized weights quantized to 1e12 and exact remainder policy are local implementation choices, not a claim of identical Proxima randomness or execution. At extreme deviations all weights can underflow; the local preview falls back to equal weights. A finite deviation bound of 1000000% is a local storage/preview bound, not a proven reference maximum. The seed is a planning seed, not a wallet key.

Optional plan.holdersDistribution is canonicalized to kind, deviationPercent and a 32-character lowercase hexadecimal seed. Older drafts without it remain readable. Owner project JSON stores it, and the legacy API uses the nullable holders_distribution TEXT column. Generated migration 0018_real_joshua_kane.sql and fresh-schema SQL were added; the migration was applied successfully only to local D1. Invalid configuration cannot overwrite either API's saved plan. Project rejection retains the prior revision and produces no history event. Existing wallet-role, revision and save guards remain independent of preview allocation.

## Holder distribution to funding handoff, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. Funding review now offers Same amount per recipient or Saved holder distribution. Distribution mode copies the current saved-plan preview's per-holder quote amounts in retained Recipient order, with an optional independently entered Deployer amount. It requires one selected Recipient for every slot, positive allocation in every slot, matching chain/currency and a captured native quote equivalent. Zero allocations, incomplete address selection and supply percentages are rejected without silently dropping slots or converting percentages. Native equivalents are explicit addresses from the existing captured catalog; token funding identity beyond native equivalents remains unsupported, even if a token has the same symbol. This is a planning handoff, not operational funding.

The existing authenticated fresh wallet-workspace fetch and active role, wallet revision and folder revision checks run before replacing the list. The existing funding validator then enforces amount precision, address uniqueness, 100-address capacity and total limits. Failures retain the entire prior draft. The fetch is abortable on unmount and denomination changes, competing editor actions remain disabled during reads, and the allocation mode is keyed to funding chain/currency. Preview transfer is a read-time check, not an atomic reservation or future execution clearance. No schema migration was required. Once saved, the funding draft remains an independent historical allocation and is not automatically rewritten by later launch changes.

## Retained custom holder weights, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. Holder distribution now offers Custom holder weights, entered as one plain non-negative decimal per holder slot. Selecting custom initially retains the current preview amounts as weights when available; otherwise it initializes equal weights. The total is normalized to those relative weights, preserving ratios when the total changes. Weights attach to slot order, not permanently to an address. The form explains this and offers an explicit reset to equal weights. Random reshuffle and deviation are disabled in custom mode. Returning to random/single modes uses their existing behavior.

Custom configuration stores canonical decimal-string weights in the existing holdersDistribution JSON. Lists support 1 to 200 values with the same local 30-whole-digit/18-fraction-place storage bounds as quote amount strings. Empty, malformed, all-zero, over-sized and holder-count-mismatched lists are rejected. Individual zero weights remain legitimate zero-allocation slots, but the existing positive-only funding handoff rejects those without dropping addresses. Weight values are relative shares rather than token amounts, so they are not limited to quote precision. Common-scale BigInt weights and exact largest remainders conserve the entered total without floating-point conversion. Percent-mode allocations remain planning percentages, not executable mint amounts. Explicit holder-count changes require explicit list correction; no silent append or truncation.

Both owner-project JSON and legacy holders_distribution TEXT persistence retain the custom weights. The shared plan parser enforces list/count compatibility, and invalid replacements preserve the prior plan and project event/revision. Older random and single configuration remains readable and does not retain irrelevant weights. No migration was needed.

Evidence: the current captured bundle SHA256 was reverified as 4284b828a9309df4b43e02bc4ecfb6a3feaf5b5d1563e89fc4f3288b253e5b58. Our decimal-string editor, exact integer rounding and all-zero rejection are local design choices. This does not implement imported key files, automatic aged-wallet assignment, preset-address matching, empty-holder generation or observed Proxima execution.

Verification: all 254 tests across 51 files passed with --maxWorkers=2. The existing actual-D1 distribution integration test now uses custom weights and confirms both APIs retain them, invalid replacements do not write or add history, and cross-owner isolation. React review confirms derived preview, explicit textarea label and event-only edits without new effects.

## Exact funding token identity, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. Funding drafts now optionally retain assetAddress from the captured chain quote-token catalog. A searchable selector shows up to 40 matching symbol/address entries, retains the selected entry even outside the filter and displays its address and captured precision. Explicit network or legacy currency edits clear the old asset identity. Amount strings are retained for review, never converted automatically. Catalog presence does not establish transfer availability, live token metadata or execution readiness.

The shared funding validator resolves the exact address within the selected chain, checks its captured symbol against currency and uses its captured decimals rather than symbol-only assumptions. EVM addresses are canonicalized to catalog case; Solana addresses remain case-sensitive. Unknown, cross-network and symbol-mismatched identities are rejected. Identity-less historical drafts retain their prior legacy currency behavior. Owner-scoped JSON funding persistence retains identity without a schema migration; invalid replacements do not mutate the document, revision or event history.

Distribution copying accepts an exact selected funding asset only when its address matches the captured launch quote asset. Without explicit identity, it retains the prior native-equivalent restriction. Token amounts are copied as exact strings with the existing fresh roster checks, positive-allocation requirement and parent funding validation. Existing chain/currency/asset keyed editor cleanup aborts superseded reads. Native-equivalent and wrapped-token planning do not imply wrapping, swapping, allowance approval or any transfer occurred.

## Retained EVM selected-contract balances, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. Wallet rows on Robinhood, BNB, Arc, Ethereum and Base now open an EVM token balance panel. Users enter 1 to 20 contract addresses or add captured chain-catalog addresses. The complete successful contract list and exact balances are retained per owner/network/wallet. This is explicit selected-contract observation, not automatic token discovery or full portfolio inventory. Symbols in the picker are captured references; decimals are read from the actual contract. Tokens lacking usable decimals are currently unsupported, even though ERC-20 makes decimals optional.

The reader validates addresses, unique contracts, list bounds, provider chain identity and fresh block headers. Read-only balanceOf and decimals calls have a 100,000 gas limit and use one blockHash with requireCanonical=true under EIP-1898. Four contract workers preserve exact uint256 strings and derive display units without floating-point conversion. ABI return words must be complete 32-byte values; decimals must fit uint8. One failed contract or provider call rejects the whole refresh and retains the earlier snapshot. No transaction is created or signed.

The owner-scoped GET and POST route uses the existing wallet_holdings_snapshots table, with max_slot serving as EVM block number. It checks saved active wallet/folder membership, shares the existing 15-second native/holdings throttle, checks wallet revision again at insertion, and rejects older blocks. Archived observations remain readable; archived wallets/folders cannot refresh. Solana holdings behavior is unchanged. No migration was needed.

## Saved EVM funding fee review, 10 October 2026

Added explicit fee review for a saved active owner-scoped Wallet funding draft on the five supported EVM networks. The API requires its exact revision, shares the existing 15-second balance/holdings gate, and checks the revision again after observation. Results are ephemeral and clear when the draft changes. No schema migration or funding-history entry is added by a review.

Native transfers and exact ERC-20 addresses are distinguished even when both are labelled ETH. Wrapped ETH is a contract transfer. Amounts and gas accounting use exact BigInt units. The reader checks network, fresh header, canonical pinned balances, and on-chain token decimals against the saved catalog. Gas estimates use the observed block number and bounded independent recipient calls; a final block-hash check rejects a reorganization. Missing transfer balance returns an explicit shortfall and unavailable fee, never an invented zero estimate. Source/recipient conflicts and unsupported source or asset profiles are rejected.

These are current independent-call gas estimates, not batch simulation, execution approval or reserve guarantees. Additional rollup data fees, token taxes, later gas prices, cumulative state effects, rebase behavior and scheduling remain outside the calculation. No automatic funding, signing, private keys, custody or transaction broadcasts were introduced. Protocol references: https://ethereum.org/developers/docs/apis/json-rpc/ and https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-1898 . EIP-1898 does not list eth_estimateGas, so this implementation uses a block number for that method and checks the final canonical header. This increment extends the funding gap documented by the retained REA investigation; no new Proxima runtime capture or fee-algorithm observation was made.

Verification: 276 tests across 54 files passed with --maxWorkers=2. Five pure reader tests cover exact native and ERC-20 calldata/amounts, balance and fee shortfalls, pinned reads, wrong network, changed block hash and decimals mismatch. Two actual-D1 tests cover saved owner revision, isolation, throttle, no history side effect, concurrent draft change and sanitized provider errors. Type checking, focused lint and production build passed. Existing build chunk warnings remain. React review confirms explicit refresh, keyed invalidation and abort cleanup.

Live Chrome proof used an isolated Base funding draft with public example funder 0x1111111111111111111111111111111111111111 and recipient 0x2222222222222222222222222222222222222222. Revision 1, native ETH, transferred one wei in the estimate: block 52400280, observed 10 October 2026 00:51:48 local, native balance 0.353821732417477845 ETH, 21000 gas, fee 0.000000126 ETH and native required 0.000000126000000001 ETH. Selecting wrapped ETH cleared that result and disabled review until saving revision 2. Contract 0x4200000000000000000000000000000000000006 then returned transfer balance 0.000323756057306993 ETH at block 52400345, observed 00:53:58 local. The one-wei ERC-20 transfer estimated 34531 gas and 0.000000207186 native ETH, with zero observed transfer/native shortfall. Provider mainnet.base.org. Only Base compatibility was verified live. These were actual read-only RPC observations without injected responses or preview-database fixtures.

## EVM token clone coverage, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. Expanded Clone token details from Solana/Robinhood to all six workspace networks. Ethereum, Base, BNB and Arc contract identity now use the same new read-only reader as Robinhood. The dedicated Robinhood research inspector remains unchanged. EVM source references validate and normalize checksummed addresses and persist in the existing owner-scoped identity JSON; no migration was needed.

The new reader verifies network ID and a fresh block header, then pins bytecode and name/symbol/decimals/totalSupply eth_call reads to a canonical block hash. Calls are gas-bounded, provider responses share the existing 512 KB limit and a 12-second deadline, and the final block header is rechecked. ABI integers preserve exact uint256 supply, including zero. Bounded dynamic strings and legacy padded bytes32 identities are supported; malformed padding, invalid UTF8/control text, absent contract, missing metadata, stale header, wrong network and reorganization fail the lookup. ERC-20 name, symbol and decimals are optional under https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-20; missing values remain unavailable rather than being fabricated. Canonical reads follow https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-1898.

This extends the previously observed Proxima clone workflow using supported local networks. No new Proxima runtime capture was made and this does not establish Proxima backend behavior. Applying a reviewed result copies name, symbol and source reference only; artwork, supply, description and socials still require review. It does not import an operational token, authorize its source address or change the destination launch network. Editing the lookup network/address aborts the previous request and clears its result.

## Token-2022 mint-side clone identity, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. Solana cloning now accepts Token-2022 metadata stored on the initialized mint. The reader uses the official Agave jsonParsed tokenMetadata and metadataPointer representation, verifies the token program owner before selecting this path, requires exactly one metadata extension and one pointer to the requested mint, and checks the embedded mint field. Name/symbol text is bounded to 256 UTF8 bytes and URI to 2048 bytes; control characters, unmatched UTF16, missing identity and contradictory linkage fail. External metadata pointers are not followed by this path. Mint supply and metadata come from the same RPC context. Exact u64 supply is retained. A mint without embedded metadata continues through the Metaplex reader. No URI or additional-metadata resource is fetched.

Lookup results now identify Token-2022, Metaplex or ERC-20 identity and show the Solana context slot. The existing explicit application copies only name, symbol and validated source reference; supply, artwork and socials remain separate draft fields. Owner-scoped source persistence uses the existing JSON schema without a migration. No new Proxima runtime capture was performed; this closes an implementation gap in the existing observed clone workflow rather than asserting reference-backend semantics.

Primary sources inspected: https://solana.com/docs/tokens/extensions/metadata ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anza-xyz/agave/master/account-decoder-client-types/src/token.rs ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anza-xyz/agave/master/account-decoder/src/parse_token_extension.rs . These establish camelCase extension/state tags, metadataAddress, mint/name/symbol/uri and embedded metadata linkage.

Verification: 306 tests across 56 files passed with --maxWorkers=2. Twelve new tests cover one-snapshot embedded identity with max-u64 supply, valid Unicode, invalid Unicode/control/byte length, mismatched mint/pointer, duplicate metadata/pointers, missing pointer/name/symbol, oversized URI, absent metadata and bounded extension lists. Existing Metaplex tests remain passing. Type checking, focused lint, production build and whitespace checks passed. Existing chunk warnings remain. React changes add only result provenance text with existing explicit lookup/application and abort cleanup.

## Multi-folder wallet membership evidence, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress through new authoritative runtime evidence that changes the next implementation. The signed-in Proxima Chrome page at https://app.proxima.tools/wallets?chain=sol was inspected again. A wallet row displayed two colored folder icons. Selecting the public-address row displayed as 8FWrz...vvs7 and opening Manage showed Assign selected wallets to folders. Its farm and Schrödinger buttons were both checked, while cook was unchecked. This is direct UI evidence that one wallet can belong to multiple folders. No folder checkbox was clicked and no membership write was submitted. The Manage popover was closed and the selected wallet was deselected; Manage was visibly disabled again. The reference UI returned to its original selection state. Screenshot: outputs/proxima-multi-folder-reference.png in the investigation workspace.

The Create drawer was also inspected without entering or generating anything. It offered Create New and Marketplace tabs, a Number of Wallets (0-100) field and an Add to folders step. The creation button remained disabled. The drawer was closed using its actual close control. This confirms visible batching and folder-assignment controls, not successful wallet generation or backend key-custody behavior.

Bundle currently contradicts multi-folder parity. Migration 0011 and db/schema.ts define one workspace_wallets.folder_id. lib/wallet-workspace.ts exposes one folderId. The wallet API reads/writes that field, and move replaces it. Wallet list counts/search/CSV/holdings availability, launch-folder selection and funding-folder selection all assume exclusive ownership by one folder. The existing external-address roster is useful but cannot represent the observed reference organization.

Concrete dependency map for the correction:

- drizzle/0011* and db/schema.ts: add owner/network/address/folder membership storage with an idempotent backfill from current folder_id. Preserve each saved address once; memberships must never duplicate addresses, balances or signing proofs.
- app/api/wallet-workspace/route.ts: expose membership IDs, owner-scoped atomic batch add/remove/replace operations, exact wallet revisions and active same-network folder checks. Existing add/edit/move/archive/restore behavior needs explicit compatibility semantics.
- components/wallet-workspace.tsx: Manage selection across multiple folders, membership-aware folder counts/search/exports, deduplicated All wallets totals and explicit archive behavior. A wallet still in an active folder must remain eligible if another folder is archived.
- app/api/wallet-workspace/balances/route.ts, holdings/route.ts, evm-holdings/route.ts and ownership/route.ts: replace exclusive folder joins with membership-aware eligibility, without duplicating snapshot rows or losing after-read revision checks.
- lib/launch-wallets.ts, lib/launch-funding.ts, components/launch/launch-wallet-picker.tsx, funding-wallet-picker.tsx and app/api/projects/route.ts: bind saved launch references to a selected active membership and its folder revision. Recheck membership when persisting or copying allocations; never count the same recipient twice through two folders.
- actual-D1 and browser QA: verify backfill, two-folder membership, owner isolation, stale wallet/folder revisions, all-or-nothing batch edits, archived membership eligibility, coherent deduplicated balances, handoff and complete reload persistence.

This requires a coordinated data-model change across roster and operational consumers, rather than a decorative multi-select. No implementation of multi-folder membership is claimed in this increment. No new REA graph or private backend capture was made; this is live DOM/screenshot evidence. Existing 306-test/build results are historical from the preceding code change and were not rerun as proof of this missing capability. Custody remains unanswered but does not block implementing multi-folder organization. Full product parity remains active and incomplete. No credentials, private keys, transfers, source-app edits, push or deployment occurred.


## Multi-folder wallet implementation verified, 10 October 2026

The earlier discovery entry describes the pre-change model. This implementation supersedes its statement that Bundle only supports one folder per wallet. Full product parity remains active and incomplete.

Migration 0019 adds persisted folder_ids and backfills existing folder_id values. Legacy nullable records still resolve to their primary membership. The local preview database migration was applied; remote migration and deployment remain outstanding. The owner-scoped wallet.folders mutation replaces memberships for up to 100 selected addresses with 1 to 100 owned, active folders on the same network. Wallet and destination-folder revisions are checked within one statement. Stale, foreign-owner and mixed-eligibility edits change none of the selected wallets.

Manage folders exposes this operation using folder checkboxes. All active wallets remains one row per address, while each folder counts its own members. Search, CSV, native balance reads, Solana/EVM holdings eligibility, ownership proof eligibility, restore, launch selection and funding recipient selection recognize memberships. Archiving the primary folder preserves eligibility through an active secondary folder. New launch references retain the explicitly selected folder ID and revision; a changed launch rejects removal of that selected membership. Unchanged historical project saves continue to retain historical references. Label/role edits preserve memberships if the primary folder is unchanged; selecting a different destination or using Move selected replaces memberships with that single folder.

Verification: 56 test files and 310 tests passed using npm run test -- --maxWorkers=2. TypeScript and production build passed. Focused lint passed for the modified wallet, launch, funding, project API and schema files. Repository-wide npm run lint exits 1 with existing reports in distribution-workbench, use-mobile and shared UI components; it is not claimed green.

Chrome QA on localhost:5173 created QA membership A and QA membership B and saved one public system-account address labelled QA shared wallet. Manage folders assigned both memberships. All active wallets counted 1 and both folders counted 1, with one table row showing both folder names. A full reload retained those values. Desktop and 390x844 mobile screenshots were saved. Archiving A left the row active through B and All active wallets remained 1. Both QA folders were then recoverably archived, retaining the row and memberships. No mainnet transaction, signing approval or private-key handling occurred.

Screenshots in the investigation workspace: outputs/bundle-multi-folder-wallets.png, outputs/bundle-multi-folder-wallets-mobile.png and outputs/bundle-secondary-folder-active.png. The source reference evidence remains outputs/proxima-multi-folder-reference.png; no new REA graph was captured for this increment.

Limits: memberships require at least one folder; unassigned/root-wallet organization remains unimplemented. The local batch UI deliberately states replace-membership semantics. The reference showed multiple checked folders, but its mixed-selection toggle/write semantics were not submitted or verified. Multi-folder storage and consumers are implemented, not complete wallet-workspace or operational parity. Production release, custody, managed wallet generation and execution integrations remain unresolved.

## Mixed-selection folder management, 10 October 2026

Previous goal turn: progress. Optional folder storage, reader eligibility, role selection and funding validation were implemented and verified with 315 tests and Chrome reload checks. This increment replaces the earlier whole-membership replacement UI with the observed per-folder toggle behavior.

Authoritative source: retained Proxima release 20261008-180532-376bdbe, page-Cw-8CN-Y.js, SHA256 4cef670e409721009dbaa9036d94817d913ef99616e2f3e060e7e903dbefd7ce. The Ho management component computes checked state with every selected wallet having that tag. Its p handler removes the target tag from all selected public keys when checked; otherwise it filters to wallets missing the target and adds it to those. The imported tag/untag hooks in index-C1cJCLGU.js update only the targeted tag, retaining unrelated tag_ids. This is shipped-client evidence; the backend write was not submitted or observed. No folder checkbox or management write was submitted. Closed Manage and deselected both rows; Manage was verified disabled.

Implementation: wallet.folder-toggle accepts one active same-network owned folder, its exact revision, a desired membership boolean and up to 100 unique owned wallet addresses with exact revisions. A single D1 statement derives each wallet's next memberships and guards every wallet and folder before applying the batch. It adds only missing memberships or removes only the target membership. Unrelated and archived memberships stay intact; primary-folder identity stays when still present, otherwise follows the first retained membership or becomes empty. Revisions/timestamps change only for wallets whose membership changes. No new migration is needed.

The Manage panel now toggles immediately, supports search and displays how many selected wallets belong to each folder. A folder is checked only if all selected wallets belong. Selection is retained after successful toggles, and current stored revisions are loaded before further edits. The old wallet.folders replacement API remains for explicit replacement consumers; Move selected and destination edits retain their documented single-destination semantics. The Manage UI no longer replaces heterogeneous memberships.

Added actual D1 tests cover heterogeneous add/remove, unchanged wallet revision preservation, primary-folder fallback, removing the final membership, stale wallet/folder batches, archived folders and foreign-owner rejection. ## Archived folders preserve parent wallet access, 10 October 2026

Previous goal turn: progress. Compact folder management and dismissal behavior were implemented and rendered on desktop and mobile. This increment corrects archive semantics using fresh signed-in reference evidence.

Source: Proxima's Archived Folders dialog says archived folders are hidden from the sidebar and their wallets stay accessible from the parent folder. This was observed in Chrome without archiving, restoring or editing any source records. The retained page-Cw-8CN-Y.js release 20261008-180532-376bdbe corroborates the copy. Screenshot: outputs/proxima-archived-folder-access.png in the investigation workspace. No new REA graph was captured.

Correction: an active wallet remains visible once in All wallets even when every organizational folder is archived. Native balance, Solana/EVM holdings and ownership eligibility depend on the owned wallet's active state and revision. Wallet restore no longer requires an active folder. Folder-specific edits still require an active owned destination. Actual wallet archives still reject new observations/proofs and launch validation; retained observations remain readable.

Parent launch references now explicitly use root=true, no folder ID and folderRevision=0. They still require the exact owned active wallet, network, role and revision. Explicit folder references retain exact active-folder and revision guards. Legacy zero-revision references without root=true remain restricted to genuinely unassigned wallets. Implementation: folder.delete accepts an owned same-network folder and its exact revision. A transactional D1 batch computes membership patches from a materialized snapshot, removes only that folder ID from affected wallets (including archived members), preserves unrelated memberships and primary identity when applicable, assigns the first remaining membership or no folder when necessary, and increments only affected wallet revisions. The second statement removes the folder with the same ownership/network/revision guard. A stale request changes nothing; a storage failure rolls the batch back. Wallet records, labels, roles, archive state and retained observations are not deleted. No new migration is required. Existing exact wallet/folder launch references will require revalidation after the membership revision change.

WalletFolderDeleteDialog is opened from the selected folder's Delete folder control. It identifies the folder, reports its member count and explains the permanent folder-only effect, with Cancel and a destructive confirmation action. Pending operations disable dismissal and show Deleting; failures stay visible within the dialog. Successful deletion reloads the workspace, closes the dialog and returns to the parent wallet list. Archived-wallet state is preserved.

## Folder sidebar menus and browser custody foundation, 10 October 2026

The previous increment delivered folder-only deletion. This increment moves Edit folder, Archive folder and Delete folder into each folder row's action menu. Fresh read-only Chrome evidence of Proxima's farm folder menu is saved as outputs/proxima-folder-actions-menu.png, alongside retained page-Cw-8CN-Y.js function Na and REA evidence ev_d8a07dc42264204ddd21cd8c3c3b1001ee8639a4a7a269c2f3a85aff0ec0fe42. No new REA graph or source write occurred. Local Base UI menus supply accessible keyboard dismissal and focus handling; exact reference hover auto-dismiss timing is not claimed.

Chrome verified that editing or opening deletion for QA membership B while QA membership A is selected targets B and preserves A's view. Cancelling returns focus to B's action trigger. Archiving B preserves A's selected view; archiving selected A returns to the four-address parent list. Both QA folders were recoverably restored for testing and returned to their original archived state. No permanent browser deletion was submitted. Mobile testing at 390x844 found left-edge popup clipping; positioner collision options now allow perpendicular fallback and keep the settled menu within left=179/right=359. The edit dialog measured left=16/right=359, cancellation returned focus to the trigger, and the viewport override/temp tabs were cleaned up. Local menu proofs are outputs/bundle-folder-actions-desktop.png and outputs/bundle-folder-actions-mobile.png.

The user selected encrypted browser custody with user-controlled export. New lib/browser-wallet-vault.ts and components/browser-wallet-vault.tsx implement one encrypted vault per browser profile and origin, independent of the signed-in server account. The UI states this scope explicitly. AES-256-GCM authenticates encrypted payloads with a fresh 96-bit IV per save and fixed format AAD. PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 uses 600,000 iterations and a random 128-bit salt. Web Crypto produces a non-extractable session encryption key. Passphrases are never saved. Envelope parsing fixes algorithm/version/iteration parameters and bounds size; decrypted payloads verify every public address against its key and reject duplicates. Native crypto.getRandomValues produces Solana seeds and EVM private keys, using existing tweetnacl/viem derivation. Generation is limited to 100 per action and 500 per vault.

Ciphertext alone is stored in localStorage. navigator.locks serializes same-origin writers and an exact prior-ciphertext comparison rejects stale tabs; unsupported locks, storage failure and cancellation fail closed. Generation encrypts and persists before exposing new public rows. Restore authenticates an encrypted backup and cannot replace an existing vault. Closing, hiding, page exit, storage changes or five minutes idle drops session references and clears passphrase fields. Byte buffers are overwritten where possible; JavaScript strings and garbage collection do not provide guaranteed memory erasure. Encryption does not protect against compromised same-origin code or an attacker controlling an unlocked browser.

The vault can add selected public addresses to the current network through the existing owner-scoped wallet.add API. No secret-bearing request is sent. EVM keys can be registered separately on EVM chains; public records remain unverified and existing execution gates stay locked. Encrypted backups are local downloads. Private-key download requires an explicit warning acknowledgement and fresh passphrase authentication, exports the whole vault (all families), and uses standard 64-byte Solana keypairs or 0x-prefixed EVM private keys. No signing, RPC or transaction entry point exists in the vault core.

Crypto references: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/AesGcmParams and https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Password_Storage_Cheat_Sheet.html. This is the chosen local architecture, not evidence of Proxima's private backend custody implementation.

Verification: 335 tests in 57 files passed. Eight new core/persistence tests cover authenticated unlock, wrong passwords, salt/IV/ciphertext tampering, non-extractable encryption keys, independently reconstructed Solana/EVM exported addresses, key/address mismatch, input bounds, stale-write protection, cancellation and quota/unsupported-lock failure. Final focused vault tests, typecheck, changed-file lint and production build passed after credential-length, accessibility and submit-button fixes. Build retains existing large-chunk and route-classification notices. Chrome observed the untouched create-vault setup form with password inputs and a submit action on desktop and mobile; mobile bounds left=16/right=359, height=601 within 390x844. Saved proofs: outputs/bundle-browser-vault-setup.png and outputs/bundle-browser-vault-mobile.png. No browser passphrase was entered or real vault created. The unlocked generate, restore, public-registration, export and auto-lock browser flows still need user-controlled end-to-end QA. Tests use disposable keys and in-memory storage, not the user's browser vault.

Product parity remains active and incomplete. Browser custody is implemented but not independently security-audited or release-proven. Marketplace fulfillment, operational signing/execution, custody recovery/import refinements, remote migration and production release remain outstanding. ## Local private-key import, 10 October 2026

Previous goal turn: progress. Folder-row menus and the encrypted browser vault were implemented with 335 tests, desktop/mobile setup evidence, and user-chosen custody. The goal remains full Proxima product parity, not just this wallet subsystem.

Retained index-C1cJCLGU.js release 20261008-180532-376bdbe includes the funder import input placeholder Enter private key (base58). Its handler passes {secret:hs.trim()} to the svm import path. This is shipped-client evidence only; no source secret import or backend execution was observed. Existing REA evidence ev_d8a07dc42264204ddd21cd8c3c3b1001ee8639a4a7a269c2f3a85aff0ec0fe42 is reused; no new graph was captured. The user chose browser-encrypted custody, so the local import deliberately does not reproduce reference server custody.

importVaultWallets accepts selected-family newline lists (64-byte Solana base58 keypairs or 32-byte EVM hex keys), a Solana CLI JSON byte array, JSON key lists, and PulseForge mixed-family private-key exports. Every key derives its public address locally. Solana keypairs verify their supplied public half against seed derivation, rather than trusting fromSecretKey's public bytes. EVM scalars reject zero/out-of-range inputs. Exported address metadata must match derivation; imported record IDs are freshly generated. Parsing and validation finish before constructing a replacement immutable payload. Existing/batch duplicates are skipped by family/address, preserving existing identities. Any malformed member rejects the entire batch without modifying the session. Input is bounded to 100 keys/100 KB and the vault stays capped at 500. Base58 strings are length-bounded before decoding. Library error messages are replaced with safe indexed format errors so supplied secrets cannot leak through error output.

The unlocked vault now has Import private keys, a labelled textarea and local .json/.txt file reader. It explains formats, all-or-nothing validation, duplicate handling and no upload. Submission clears the sensitive input, encrypts the resulting vault, commits through the existing Web Locks/previous-ciphertext guard, then publishes only public rows and selects newly imported addresses for the selected family. Import, export and lock transitions clear the textarea. File/visibility/cancellation races cannot commit after the session has locked. Mixed exports can import both families; public registration still targets only the selected network. An unlock now rejects a vault that disappeared from storage instead of creating a replacement without confirmation. Public registration shows the selected count/100-address batch limit.

Verification: 15 focused vault tests passed, including seven import cases. They cover independently derived Solana JSON/base58 identities, EVM prefix/case handling, duplicate preservation, mixed export portability, regenerated IDs, metadata mismatch, invalid second-member atomicity, generic secret-free error text, malformed keypair public halves, invalid byte ranges, invalid EVM scalars, input/export-family bounds, and duplicate-vs-new imports at capacity. A 500-wallet ciphertext is below the envelope size cap and decrypts to the exact public inventory. Typecheck, focused lint and production build pass. The historical full-suite result remains 335 tests/57 files from the previous increment; no new full-suite run is claimed. The current focused file contains 15 tests, replacing its prior eight-test count.

## Vault-backed ownership verification, 10 October 2026

Previous goal turn: progress. Local private-key import was implemented and tested. Full product parity remains the objective. This increment connects user-chosen browser custody to historical public-wallet ownership proofs; it does not claim that Proxima uses the same proof workflow or custody implementation.

The owner-scoped /api/wallet-workspace/ownership route now supports Solana Ed25519 signatures alongside five EVM networks. Challenge messages come from one shared canonical builder and identify this site's origin, network, address, nonce, issuance, expiry and ownership-only purpose. Solana challenges identify Ed25519 and have a null EVM chain ID. issuedAt is returned explicitly and expiry is exactly five minutes after that issuance. Verification uses the retained server message, rejects another site, recovers EVM addresses or verifies Solana detached signatures, then consumes the nonce and inserts the historical proof in a transaction. Existing owner/network/active-wallet checks, replay rejection, challenge caps and rollback guards remain. Organizational folders do not determine key ownership. No schema migration is required.

New lib/vault-ownership.ts exposes only signVaultOwnership. It accepts the exact canonical ownership challenge for the selected key, current origin and network. It rejects arbitrary payloads, extra instructions, mismatched addresses/chain IDs, missing keys, expired messages and excessive/future lifetimes before signing. EVM uses an EIP-191 ownership message; Solana uses Ed25519. Derivation rechecks the public address against the retained key. Byte buffers/keypair arrays are cleared where possible, while the existing JavaScript-memory limitation remains. No transaction or arbitrary-message signer is exposed. The external EVM connector now applies the same strict challenge validation.

The unlocked vault lists a Verify ownership action for public addresses already saved on the selected network. Preparing a challenge does not sign. A separate Review ownership message panel shows the exact message and a Sign ownership message and verify action or cancellation. Only the public address, challenge ID and resulting signature are submitted to this site. A verified result reloads the roster and displays the existing historical Signature verified badge. Lock, hide, close, storage changes and chain unmount cancel pending client work and clear the review state. Aborting a request cannot retract a proof already accepted by the server; no transaction is authorized by such a proof. Setup copy now distinguishes permitted ownership messages from unavailable transaction signing/execution.

Verification: all 350 tests in 58 files passed. Four restricted-signer tests verify restored Solana signatures, independently recover EVM addresses for all five network messages, and reject other sites/addresses/networks/chain IDs, arbitrary/altered content and invalid expiry fields. Four new real D1 Solana integration cases use disposable imported keys to exercise the vault signer, route verifier and roster proof reader; they reject wrong keys, altered text, another site or authenticated owner, malformed signatures, expiry, archive and replay. Concurrent requests admit one proof. A forced proof INSERT failure rolls back nonce consumption and retains the prior proof count. Existing EVM ownership tests continue passing. Typecheck, focused changed-file lint, diff whitespace check and production build passed. Existing build chunk/route-classification notices remain.

Chrome observed the updated setup wrapper with ownership-message scope. Screenshot outputs/bundle-vault-ownership-setup.png is setup evidence, not a successful browser signature. The unlocked browser review/sign/generate/import/export flows still require user-controlled end-to-end QA. ## Wallet warmup planning and history, 10 October 2026

User requested wallet warmup and selected both planning and history checks. My wallets now opens Wallet warmup for 1 to 20 selected active public addresses. A local review plan accepts 1 to 10 token addresses, 1 to 20 buy/sell cycles, a fixed interval, future start, exact native buy amount and per-wallet fee reserve. Deterministic staggered buys and matching 100 percent acquired-token sells are scheduled for review only. BigInt budgets count every buy and the reserve without relying on proceeds. Input/selection/revision changes invalidate review. JSON export contains public planning data and executionAllowed=false. Plans are session-local with export; there is no saved-plan server table, scheduler, quote, token-identity/liquidity verification or swap signing.

The new authenticated warmup history route verifies active ownership, same-origin requests, bounded selection, shared 15-second wallet-read gate, and unchanged wallet revisions after RPC. Up to three readers run concurrently with a 25-second request timeout and per-wallet sanitized failure results. Solana checks mainnet genesis and observes finalized balance plus latest 50 signatures, successful sample count and oldest/latest dated sampled activity. EVM checks chain ID and canonical block, then reads exact balance/account nonce pinned to that block. Neither establishes wallet creation age; EVM does not provide incoming/internal history. Provider source is hostname only. No token trades are inferred from signatures/nonces.

## Native SOL unsigned funding preparation, 10 October 2026

Immediate saved Solana wallet-source drafts now prepare native SOL legacy System Program transfer messages with zero placeholder signatures. The server checks mainnet genesis, finalized account contexts, ordinary system-wallet accounts, exact lamport budgets, recipient rent minimums, aggregate transfer fees and funder rent reserve. It quotes each exact message, simulates without signature verification, then rechecks blockhash validity and last valid height. Contexts below the requested minimum or more than 32 slots apart are rejected. No signing or sending method is present. Token funding, future schedules and recipient delays remain unsupported by this preparation path.

The SOL panel exports public unsigned JSON for at most 60 seconds; field edits invalidate preparation. ## Devnet approval boundary and warmup verification, 10 October 2026

Existing connected-wallet devnet actions trusted their configured RPC URL. They now check getGenesisHash before balance/preflight reads and immediately before calling the SDK transaction workflow. The expected hash was verified against https://api.devnet.solana.com. Wrong, unavailable or unresponsive identity checks fail closed; the check has a ten-second deadline and cancellation. This protects against an incorrectly configured cluster, not a malicious RPC fabricating its identity.

Prepared memo and SPL actions last 60 seconds, bind to the wallet and operation revision, and are consumed before calling the transaction workflow. SPL preflight also binds to the raw manifest, supply, reserve, review-group size and mint. Returning to earlier inputs does not revive an old review. Pending provider results cannot restore state or request approval after an input change or unmount. A source token account must match the connected owner and be initialized and unfrozen. Each next SPL transfer requires a fresh preflight. Already-open wallet prompts cannot be cancelled by these guards; a transfer completing after an edit retains its receipt under the original manifest. Local distribution receipts still require independent chain verification. Browser-vault transaction signing remains incomplete.

## Exact token distribution amounts, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. The devnet distribution planner previously parsed CSV amounts and merged duplicates as JavaScript numbers. This could reject decimal sums such as 0.1 plus 0.2 during mint conversion or round large amounts before review. Amounts, supply and reserve inputs now remain decimal strings. BigInt arithmetic merges duplicates, calculates totals, compares the rational reserve allowance exactly and converts to the mint's base units. Mint precision and SPL u64 overflow reject the action. Number conversion is confined to the visual allocation percentage; source balances, review-group totals and recipient/receipt amounts display every decimal digit. New audit manifests are version 2 with decimal-string encoding. This is our execution foundation, not a claim of observed reference implementation behavior.

Preflight now budgets only remaining recipients. The earlier all-recipient balance check could block a second transfer after the first depleted the source account. Existing numeric local receipts are normalized compatibly so completed recipients are not automatically repeated after this amount-format change. New receipts store strings. Malformed, oversized or unreadable receipt collections block preparation rather than silently becoming an empty collection. A confirmed transfer whose local write fails is identified as confirmed with a storage failure, and further approval is blocked. Local receipt validation remains shape validation, not independent chain verification; cross-tab coordination and durable server-side operation history remain outstanding.

Bounds: amounts use up to 20 whole digits and nine decimal places, mint decimals are 0 through 9, review groups are integer sizes 1 through 12, and manifests have a 262144-character cap with at most 1000 data entries. Reserve comparisons retain sub-token precision rather than adding a floating point tolerance. Existing imported JSON is not rewritten, and legacy local receipt keys are retained.

Verification: all 419 tests in 69 files passed, along with final type checking, five-file lint, formatting and production build. The 14 distribution/receipt tests cover decimal duplicate merging, tiny amounts, large exact values, serialization, fractional reserve boundaries, u64 limits, mint precision, malformed controls, legacy receipt continuity, remaining budgets and corrupt-receipt rejection. Independent installed SDK instruction encoding checks exact bytes for 0.3 at nine decimals, 9007199254740993 at zero decimals and maximum u64 at nine decimals.

Chrome verified a merged total of 0.300000001, exact review of 9007199254740993.000000001 and rejection of 0.300000001 against a 0.3 reserve allowance. Both exports downloaded successfully; a browser download-observation timeout was resolved by inspecting the generated artifacts, without repeating the first export. The version-2 large-value export is retained as outputs/bundle-distribution-exact-audit.json. Desktop/mobile proofs are outputs/bundle-distribution-exact-amounts.png and outputs/bundle-distribution-exact-mobile.png. At 390px the review measured left=14/right=361/client=scroll=347, without panel overflow. The temporary viewport was reset and disposable inputs were cleared. No wallet connection, signing, transfer, source-app write, migration, push or deployment occurred. Full Proxima parity remains active and incomplete.

## Durable pending devnet operations, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. Added an account-owned operation journal before connected-wallet approval. Migration 0023 stores the exact memo/SPL intent, a hashed session claim, one-time approval state, optional returned signature and a confirmed preparation slot. Only the existing local preview database was migrated. The slot field was added locally after initial migration testing and is included in the final fresh-schema migration. No actual operation rows were inserted into the preview database.

An atomic partial unique index permits one unresolved operation per account and wallet across memo and distribution consoles. Preparing reservations last 60 seconds and can be cancelled with their session claim before approval. An attempted approval consumes that reservation. Armed or submitted operations do not expire automatically; a lost response, browser reload or different browser cannot silently release the wallet. The session claim is never returned in history. Retention is bounded to 1000 operations per account, with the latest 50 per wallet and a separate active-operation lookup.

Both connected-wallet submission paths now reserve, arm, recheck their current reviewed inputs and only then invoke the existing SDK workflow. A returned signature is preserved locally before registration, and server registration still runs if a local receipt write fails. A stale-input failure before arming cancels the preparation; after an uncertain arm response the operation is retained. This does not cancel an already-open wallet prompt or recover a signature the SDK never returned.

Arming verifies the devnet genesis and reads a confirmed slot with a ten-second deadline. Ownership and reservation eligibility are rechecked after that provider read. Receipt recovery must match the wallet, operation kind and exact transfer intent, and its finalized transaction slot must be newer than the preparation slot. A transaction signature cannot finalize two operations. This uses the chain slot rather than comparing estimated block time with the server clock. Existing matching receipt retries also reconcile operation state without another RPC read. A failed, mismatched or older receipt does not release an armed operation. The reservation endpoint also checks existing finalized recipient receipts, preventing a stale tab from repeating a completed distribution recipient after another tab resolves its lock.

Added a shared pending-operation panel, reload action and receipt-only recovery using a retained signature or a manually supplied existing signature. The panel and preparation/submission controls hold on unavailable operation history. Ownership message verification remains available while an operation is unresolved. Desktop Chrome verified the new panel and disabled preflight/approval on a valid disposable manifest without a connected wallet. Proof: outputs/bundle-pending-operation-guard.png. Disposable inputs were cleared afterward.

Verification: all 450 tests in 72 files passed after the confirmed-slot correction. This includes 17 real-D1 wallet API tests, five client sequencing tests and the preparation-provider regression. Coverage includes competing reservations, owner and session isolation, expiry, one-time arming, provider failure before approval, known signature registration, idempotent receipt reconciliation, an older-slot rejection, exact-intent matching, stale-tab duplicate rejection and preserving uncertainty after a lost response. Type checking, focused lint, formatting and production build passed.

Limitations: an approval started without a returned signature remains unresolved until an existing matching transaction can be supplied. Verified failed-transaction release, explicitly rejected wallet prompt reconciliation and operator recovery are not implemented. The SDK may broadcast before returning its signature, so signatureless broadcast recovery is still incomplete. Real extension approval and connected recovery were not exercised. Direct JSON navigation in Chrome was blocked by its client and was not bypassed. A 390px viewport override left the measured desktop layout unchanged, so the new panel's mobile rendering remains unverified; the override was reset. That capture is labelled outputs/bundle-pending-operation-viewport-unverified.png. No wallet connection, vault unlock, user signature, transaction, reference-app write, code push or deployment occurred. Full Proxima parity remains active and incomplete.

Protocol reference: https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/getslot . This is our operation coordination design, not a claim of captured Proxima backend behavior.

## Verified failed devnet operation recovery, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. The previous goal turn verified warmup planning/history, the full 450-test suite, type checking and production build. This increment addresses the pending journal's finalized-failure gap. Receipt outcome is derived only from devnet finalized transaction metadata. Supported transaction-error variants and instruction errors require the same reviewed fee payer, message and exact memo/SPL intent. Integer-string RPC parsing is normalized, and native balances use exact u64 BigInt arithmetic. A failed transaction must change native balances only by its reported fee and leave reviewed token balances unchanged. Missing, unsupported, inconsistent, old-slot or unrelated evidence retains the reservation. Absent token accounts can be admitted only with matching intent and complete native rollback evidence.

Failure receipts use memo_failed/token_transfer_failed and retain the normalized transaction error, finalized slot and reported fee in lamports. The operation resolver transitions an eligible reservation to failed, with the existing owner, newer-slot, exact-intent and unique-signature checks. Repeating verification is idempotent. The existing active-operation partial index already excludes terminal states, so no migration is required. Failed transfers do not satisfy the completed-recipient guard; another attempt requires a new operation and fresh review. One failed signature cannot resolve another operation.

The shared recovery panel handles both outcomes. Distribution history continues to count only token_transfer_finalized as delivered, while a locally retained signature with verified failed history stops blocking the next preflight. Corrupted local storage still holds preparation. The memo console labels failure explicitly, resolves its pending cache and requires fresh preflight. Activity displays requested amounts as not delivered and shows the reported fee rather than describing a failed receipt as finalized success.

Verification: all 458 tests in 73 files passed.
