## Signature capture before devnet broadcast, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. The previous goal turn implemented finalized-failure recovery and passed 458 tests. The installed RPC executor signs a transaction before invoking sendAndConfirmTransaction. The prior application callback ran only after that complete SDK call returned, leaving a lost broadcast/confirmation response without a retained signature. Both memo and standard SPL submission paths now construct an operation-local SDK client using the same RPC/subscription objects, payer, resource estimation, compute-unit price and single-transaction planner. A tracked transaction-signing wrapper registers the signed identity before returning to the SDK broadcaster.

The wrapper permits one transaction and one reviewed-wallet signer. It snapshots the reviewed message and blockhash lifetime, verifies exact returned bytes/lifetime and a cryptographically valid Ed25519 signature, and owns a codec-round-tripped copy of returned buffers while registration is pending. It preserves the signature even when inputs changed during an open wallet prompt, but prevents app broadcasting after that change. Lost registration acknowledgement, invalid signatures, changed messages and local storage failure prevent the signed transaction from reaching the app broadcaster. Local failure still attempts durable signature registration. A signing-only capability is required before reserving the operation; combined-send-only wallets receive an explicit unsupported message and are not silently routed through the old path.

Owned operation sequencing now passes an awaited signature recorder to the signing flow. It records one identity once, requires matching server id/state/signature acknowledgement and rejects a second transaction identity. The local and durable identifiers therefore exist before an app broadcast is attempted, including if the subsequent network response is lost. The existing submitted state represents a registered signature and does not prove broadcast or delivery. The pending panel explicitly labels that uncertainty. Local distribution-cache capture is also described as signed rather than confirmed; completed counts remain based solely on independently verified successful receipts.

The already-installed instruction-plan plugin version 0.13.0 is now a direct exact dependency for composing the operation-local SDK client. No installed package versions changed. The first offline npm metadata lookup was unavailable; the exact-version registry install succeeded. This implementation follows installed SDK source, not inferred Proxima backend behavior.

Verification: all 467 tests in 74 files passed. The focused 14-test signer and operation-sequencing regression covers installed memo/SPL builders and Kit signing, acknowledgement ordering, rejection, changed message/lifetime, invalid signatures, stale input after signing, combined-send-only refusal, lost registration/broadcast response, local storage failure and duplicate-identity rejection. Type checking, focused lint, formatting, whitespace checking and production build passed. Existing build notices remain.

No real extension approval, RPC broadcast or connected recovery was performed. A wallet prompt with no returned signed transaction, an app crash before capture, unsupported wallets, rejected-prompt reconciliation and a registered signed transaction that never lands still require further recovery work. No private keys or signed transaction bytes are persisted by this change. Browser-vault signing, scheduling, mainnet/venue adapters and broader Proxima operations remain incomplete. No wallet connection, vault unlock, user signature, funds movement, source-app write, migration, push or deployment occurred. The full product goal remains active.

## Explicit devnet signing rejection recovery, 10 October 2026

Previous goal turn: progress, with an implemented browser-vault connection, cryptographic fixture evidence and verified disconnected Chrome controls. This increment closes the known rejected-prompt retry gap for the existing signing-only paths.

The tracked signer marks only a numeric 4001 thrown by its signing invocation or our explicit vault cancellation marker. It does not search error text, unwrap arbitrary causes, accept string codes, or interpret broadcaster/registration errors as signing rejection. A previously captured signature prevents automatic release. A cancelled vault request cannot return a transaction to the app broadcaster. Phantom documents numeric 4001 as user rejection: https://docs.phantom.com/solana/errors . Other wallet error encodings remain unsupported and retained as uncertain outcomes.

The originating approval session can report this explicit rejection and atomically cancel an awaiting-signature reservation only while its signature is null. Wrong-owner/claim requests fail, prepared and submitted operations cannot use this transition, and rejection is serialized against signature registration. Repeated acknowledged cancellation is idempotent. This is a trusted client/session report, not independently verified chain absence. No new manual release button or expiry for uncertain approvals was added. A lost or inconsistent release acknowledgement tells the user to refresh pending operations before retrying. Existing consoles refresh their journal after either outcome; a new attempt still needs fresh review and reservation.

No browser signing prompt or user-vault unlock was performed. Actual extension rejection and vault cancellation during a connected transaction remain unverified in Chrome. No migration, reference write, transfer, push or deployment occurred. Full Proxima parity remains active, including broader live adapters, funding, run management, warmup execution/scheduling, marketplace and release QA.

Verification: all 482 tests in 75 files passed. Nine new regressions cover signer rejection classification, downstream error isolation, capture retention, uncertain acknowledgement, owner/session isolation, fresh reservation eligibility, rejection/registration races and previously submitted signature retention. Final TypeScript checking, focused lint, formatting, whitespace checking and production build passed. Existing build notices remain.

## Saved native SOL funding execution on devnet, 10 October 2026

Previous goal turn: progress, with explicit signing rejection recovery and 482 passing tests. This increment adds the first execution handoff from a saved native funding draft. It is an implemented devnet path, not a claim that Proxima's backend or full mainnet funding behavior has been reproduced.

Immediate native SOL wallet-source drafts with zero delay now expose a separate devnet funding console. It requires the connected address to match the saved funder, current owner verification and a fresh per-recipient review. The budget uses exact lamports for remaining recipients, a 6400-lamport fee allowance per transfer and the provider's zero-data rent minimum. Funder and recipient must be ordinary System accounts; a new recipient must reach rent exemption. Inconsistent funder balances or context-slot spread over 150 slots reject preparation. These are bounded preparation checks; they do not guarantee future fees, balance or network availability.

Reservations load the authenticated owner's active saved draft and require its exact funder, amount, recipient and revision. The eligibility condition is rechecked atomically during reservation and after the provider read when arming. A per-recipient fingerprint includes devnet, funding ID, wallet, recipient and canonical amount, excluding the revision; naming or revision changes do not revive an already-funded identical row. A changed amount represents a new explicitly reviewed transfer amount. Another unresolved memo, SPL or native operation blocks this wallet. Changed local inputs/wallet, expiry or unmount invalidate the review. Wallet results finishing later still retain the original operation's signature before the app broadcaster is allowed to receive a transaction.

Native transfers use the installed System SDK builder through the tracked signing-only client. The vault approval checks actual decoded native transfer bytes against the reserved amount and recipient, and identifies SOL explicitly. The private key remains subject to separate passphrase approval. A pending signature is saved locally under its original wallet before server registration, with registration still attempted after local storage failure. Reload and the shared journal offer receipt-only recovery. Inaccessible/corrupt local pending storage holds new preparation. No signed transaction bytes or private keys are stored by this handoff.

Receipt verification uses the retained owner-scoped operation intent, finalized devnet evidence, one ordinary native transfer, one wallet signer, exact amount/destination and effective native balance changes including the fee. Additional instruction effects, inner/token effects, missing balances and mismatches reject admission. Supported finalized failure must show fee-only rollback and leaves the recipient unpaid. Verified success/failure details include saved funding identity, amount, slot and fee; Activity labels each distinctly. Receipts can resolve the original operation after a later draft change, while new approvals still require the current revision. Saved-draft status reads derive paid rows from matching finalized owned receipts. No migration is needed.

Verification: all 493 tests in 76 files passed. Eleven new tests cover native intent/fingerprint validation, exact and large lamport budgets, rent/account/slot rejection, installed SDK vault signing, native success/failure balance evidence, owned saved-draft admission, changed revision, repeat prevention and failed recipient recovery. The Activity rendering regression also covers unpaid native amounts. Final TypeScript checking, focused lint, formatting and whitespace checking passed. Production build passed; existing build notices remain. Added direct exact dependency @solana-program/system 0.13.0, already present transitively; installed package versions did not change.

Chrome created a disposable public-address QA draft with one 0.300000001 SOL recipient, saved it, fully reloaded/reopened it and verified exact retained itemization, 0/1 completed receipts and disabled ownership/preparation/approval without a connected wallet. Proof before archiving: outputs/bundle-devnet-native-funding.png. The QA draft was archived recoverably at revision 2, with the archive notice and row verified. An initial HMR navigation had a stale vault context, and HMR temporarily duplicated unsigned panels; full reload restored the provider and one-panel source layout. No user vault unlock, wallet selection, ownership signature, approval, transfer, source-app write, migration, push or deployment occurred. Connected native execution and failure/reload QA, mobile rendering, mainnet/EVM funding, saved-draft SPL execution, scheduling and broader parity remain incomplete. The full goal remains active.

Protocol references: https://solana.com/docs/core/instructions and https://solana.com/docs/rpc/json-structures . These establish instruction and transaction-evidence structure; our matching and recovery rules are implementation choices.

## Devnet funding simulation before approval, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. The previous goal turn added saved funding execution history. This increment checks the next native SOL or standard SPL transfer through the configured devnet RPC before publishing a prepared approval. Full product parity remains active and incomplete.

Preparation builds the installed SDK transfer/ATA instructions using an address-only signer whose signing and signature-registration callbacks throw. The compiled unsigned transaction contains zero placeholder signatures, a current confirmed blockhash and a bounded 1,400,000-unit review ceiling. Devnet genesis is checked; fee quotation and simulation run in parallel with a minimum context slot. Simulation explicitly disables signature verification and blockhash replacement, and never invokes a transaction broadcaster. A quoted fee over the existing 6,400-lamport per-transfer allowance, null fee, program error, missing/excessive consumed units, inconsistent reported simulation fee, backwards/stale context or expired blockhash holds approval. Provider calls use an abort signal and a 20-second simulation deadline. The overall prepared review expires 60 seconds after preparation started, including account reads and simulation time.

The UI publishes no prepared state until the complete simulation and lifetime checks pass, and its send guard also requires simulation evidence. The prepared panel reports the simulated slot, quoted fee at the review compute ceiling and consumed units. Signing still refreshes the blockhash and resource estimate; simulation does not reserve balances or prove future delivery. The existing saved-revision gate, wallet ownership, exact recipient/mint/amount intent, per-wallet reservation, signature capture and independently verified receipts remain in force.

Protocol references retrieved this turn: https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/simulatetransaction and https://solana.com/docs/rpc/http/getfeeformessage . Official RPC documentation supports simulation without a signature when sigVerify is false, without broadcasting. Installed Kit API declarations and SDK transaction builders are authoritative for local typed integration. This is our preparation design, not a claim about captured Proxima backend behavior.

Verification: the full suite passed 512 tests in 80 files. The final focused five-test simulation regression passed after the minimum-context guard was tightened. It decodes the actual unsigned SOL/SPL SDK wire transactions and verifies them against the existing vault intent inspector, including canonical ATA creation, exact amounts and zero signatures. Failure cases cover wrong genesis, changed review, unavailable/excessive fees, backwards/stale slots, simulation errors, invalid consumed units, expired blockhash and stalled-provider abort. An initial full run timed out in the existing history pagination test under concurrent build/browser load; that test now seeds verified fixture evidence in one batch rather than repeating ownership/reservation/receipt setup unrelated to pagination, preserving its 51-row/tie/cursor/corruption assertions. The subsequent full suite passed. Final TypeScript checking, focused lint, formatting and whitespace checks passed; production build passed with the existing deprecation, chunk-size, plugin timing and route-classification notices.

## Imported-project revision history, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. Previous turn implemented persistent existing-token projects. This increment exposes their already-atomic saved events in an authenticated owner-scoped history endpoint and the imported details panel. Archived projects remain readable. ## Wallet-bound unsigned swap preparation, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. Imported Solana buy/sell controls now request a wallet-bound Jupiter Swap V2 order through the shared exact-amount quote core. This uses the official GET /order taker contract, requests transaction version 0 and excludes JupiterZ; sponsored/multiple-signer orders are unsupported in this first preparation adapter. The new authenticated same-origin bounded prepare endpoint derives mint and decimals from the active owned project, checks the active owned wallet revision, validates exact amounts, shares the import read gate, and rechecks project/wallet revisions and order expiry after provider work. Missing server configuration returns 503 before provider access. Failures are sanitized. Reads do not create events, operations or execution authority.

The unsigned review decodes a canonical base64 Solana packet capped at 1232 bytes, round-trips both transaction and compiled v0 message to reject trailing/noncanonical material, requires the requested sole writable signer and unsigned signature, validates header/account index bounds and no duplicate static accounts, and rejects sponsored fees. Safe integer signature/priority/rent estimates are summed with BigInt. It retains a transaction SHA-256 fingerprint, envelope counts and last-valid block height. It explicitly sets executionAllowed=false and instructionsVerified=false: this is envelope verification, not swap instruction, debit, lookup-table, blockhash, RPC fee or simulation validation. A real unsigned system-transfer fixture is deliberately accepted as an envelope in tests and never claimed to be a verified swap. This distinction must remain until semantic validation and simulation are implemented.

UI binds preparation to exact project/wallet revisions, direction, amount and mint pair, clears it on changes/unmount, displays expiry and provider fee estimates, and exposes no signing/export/submit action. ## Simulation token balance comparisons, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. Independent unsigned Solana simulation now reads pre/post token metadata from the RPC response and resolves account indices against static keys followed by loaded writable and readonly lookup keys. For non-native input, the report compares the observed net token debit with the requested exact input. For non-native output, it compares net token increase with the quote minimum. ## Persistent warmup history runs, 10 October 2026

Classification: progress. The previously REA-analyzed shipped useLiveWalletFeatureRuns module exposes Planned/Running/Paused/Cancelled/Completed/Failed labels and pause/resume/cancel controls (evidence ev_d8a07dc42264204ddd21cd8c3c3b1001ee8639a4a7a269c2f3a85aff0ec0fe42). Its private backend remains unknown. Bundle now implements its own owned run lifecycle for the user's explicitly requested public warmup history checks. This is a real RPC review workflow, not background trade automation or fabricated execution.

Migration 0028 adds owner/plan-scoped run state, optimistic revision, immutable captured plan/wallet versions, retained observations, creation/update times and a private read lease nonce. A partial unique index admits one active run per plan; account storage is bounded to 100 runs and listing returns the latest 20 per plan. Each explicit step performs one existing chain-verified public history read with a 25-second deadline and shared 15-second owner read throttle. A 45-second lease permits retry after an interrupted worker/request, while nonce/revision admission prevents a late prior read from overwriting recovered progress. Pause and cancel invalidate an in-flight lease. Atomic SQL checks the active captured plan revision and all saved wallet revisions before and after reads; edits/archive races discard the observation, retain prior results and pause the still-owned lease. Provider failures are stored explicitly without readiness conclusions. Completed means all requested checks finished, including failures.

The saved-plan UI lazily opens history runs, creates, pauses, resumes, cancels and advances them, reloads stored results and preserves archived-plan access. Closing or changing context aborts pending browser requests. During a slow read, explicit reload retrieves the current revision before pause/cancel. Lease eligibility is based on the last successful reload time and asks the user to reload after expiry. No polling, scheduler, signer or swap execution is enabled. This increment does not implement a full operational automation engine or per-transition event timeline.

Verification: 12 focused tests across the new actual-D1 API suite and existing history-reader suite passed. Eight run tests cover owned transitions/terminal states, no RPC on create, active uniqueness, auth/origin/content-type/version checks, pause and cancellation during RPC, wallet edits during RPC, expired lease recovery, a late old response after recovery, explicit provider failure retention, partial progress/throttling and archived-plan reads. ## Durable warmup buy and sell reviews, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress. Saved Solana warmup plans now retain unsigned buy/sell preparation summaries, bound to the plan revision, cycle, wallet revision and transaction fingerprint. Atomic insertion requires the exact current plan document and all captured wallet revisions to remain active. It verifies the selected cycle/mint/wallet and exact saved buy budget, rejects expired quotes and caps history at 200 records per plan. A full history is rejected before provider work, and a failed save cannot return an unrecorded prepared order. Inventory-only reads remain separate. Authenticated history reads support archives, twenty-record cursor pages and tied-timestamp ordering; cursors must refer to a row owned by the same plan. Closing/reopening or explicitly reloading the panel reads saved summaries, without asking a provider for another order.

## Guarded buy-to-sell receipts and user-approved warmup signing, 11 October 2026

Added finalized exact-packet sell receipt verification, standard SPL net debit checks, and fee-adjusted native plus wrapped-SOL output checks. Receipt storage consumes recorded buy units using canonical BigInt strings and an atomic D1 compare-and-swap batch. Unique preparation/signature indexes prevent duplicate consumption. Subsequent linked sell reviews use remaining units; independent current holdings are still checked. External transfers and pending external transactions are not reconstructed, and native output includes rent effects, so this ledger is not proof of profit or fungible token provenance.

The first mainnet signing adapter requires a single matched Jupiter Metis route, existing standard SPL source/destination accounts without delegates or separate close authorities, complete simulated input/output metadata, no unrelated token changes, exact native fee-only debit, independently matching compute fees capped at 1,000,000 lamports, and no lookup tables. Only Jupiter and supported compute-budget instructions are allowed. Wrapping, token-account creation/closure and other top-level instructions remain blocked. Thus native SOL setup/cleanup, ALT routes, Token-2022 and external-wallet signing are outstanding; the current adapter uses existing wrapped SOL. Simulation and pinned route fields are observations, not a guarantee of deployed CPI behavior, execution or profit.

The server reconstructs quote fields from the owner-scoped saved preparation, rechecks the original packet fingerprint, current plan and all captured wallet revisions, quota-derived history bounds, fresh mainnet simulation and remaining sell units. Review creates a durable approval reservation, with one active transaction per owner/wallet. The browser requires an explicit risk/amount/network checkbox and vault passphrase, signs the immutable bytes locally, clears its unlocked session, and registers the exact verified signature before offering a separate submit button. Passphrases and private keys never go to the signing API. Submission rechecks current state and simulation, marks the reservation submitted before one bounded send with preflight and no automatic retries. A timeout retains the signature/reservation; it never enables a replacement automatically.

Retained preparation history can read approval status, cancel unsigned reservations and check registered finalized outcomes. A verified finalized failure releases the reservation. Successful transactions require the matching buy/sell receipt verifier to finalize accounting. Registered buy receipt recovery can finish after plan archival/revision changes; ordinary new receipt checks retain their existing revision guards. Signing records retain public unsigned packet bytes for exact recovery; the earlier preparation history remains summary-only.

Local-only migrations 0044 (six commands) and 0045 (four commands) applied successfully. 57 tests across six focused files passed, including cryptographic exact-packet signing, altered signer/hash/expiry rejection, restricted instruction/debit eligibility, simultaneous sell consumption, idempotency, owner isolation, archived registered-buy recovery, durable signature registration, submission timeout and rejected duplicate sends, and finalized failure recovery. RPC responses and eligible-route assessment are mocked in submission lifecycle fixtures; they are not live-trade evidence. Typecheck, targeted lint and production build passed. Build retains known chunk-size/deprecation/route-classification warnings. Chrome verified the archived sell receipt form and empty history state; screenshot: bundle-warmup-sell-workflow.png in this chat workspace. No live vault unlock, signature, broadcast, swap, remote migration or deployment was performed. Live provider quote and end-to-end transaction QA remain outstanding. ## Native SOL settlement and current Jupiter route support, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress. Warmup buy preparation, approval and receipt accounting now support exact native SOL wrapping, standard SPL associated-account setup, supported seeded temporary accounts, SyncNative, and wallet-directed wrapped-SOL cleanup after a buy or sell. The adapter validates canonical derived addresses, owners, mint/precision, setup order and exact input. It rejects extra transfers, redirected refunds, authority changes, unsupported top-level programs, incomplete simulation accounts and unrelated token debits. Observed account rent is budgeted separately from swap proceeds. Complete closure accepts either an absent account or a zero-lamport, empty, non-executable system account, as observed from the live RPC.

Review records bind all static and lookup-derived account keys plus each independently read lookup-table fingerprint. Submission rechecks the exact approved packet, rent, fee and table observations before one bounded send. Final receipts must match the registered packet and approved loaded addresses. Buy accounting retains only net acquired units; finalized matching sells atomically reduce remaining units and exclude account rent and old wrapped-SOL refunds from swap output. Unknown submission outcomes keep the registered signature reserved and do not enable an automatic retry or replacement.

The route decoder now reads ordinary and shared exact-input Jupiter V1/V2 headers, including V2 basis-point route weights and bounded compound/candidate variants. It validates connected route graphs, exact wallet/mints/input, minimum-output protection and token destinations. The pinned published program IDL at slot 454222193 contains 197 Swap variants and 19 candidate variants. Its SHA-256 is 82a3907455712fa9c2104d1b8fb8a2649cc4d96789d4c16a572ed343981b9f4e. Layout tests cover every captured variant in both route formats; this is structural coverage, not proof that every venue or deployed CPI path was exercised live. Schema provenance points to the published Solscan IDL linked by Jupiter's developer announcement.

V2 platform fees must match the saved quote exactly, use input or output mint, and remain at or below 50 bps. Positive-slippage fees are rejected. The captured live order declared a 2 bps SOL fee. This metadata is retained with the immutable owned preparation and reconstructed for fresh approval. The browser review displays it alongside fees, wrapping, rent, upfront SOL debit and pinned table count. Each transaction still requires the user's explicit amount/risk review, local vault passphrase and a separate submit action.

The user created the dedicated Bundle Local Development Jupiter key. It was saved privately in ignored server-only .env.local and both actual SOL-to-USDC and USDC-to-SOL quotes returned HTTP 200. A separate direct fresh unsigned settlement review passed: 2000000 lamports wrapped, 2976880 maximum new-account rent, 5103751 maximum upfront SOL debit, wallet-directed cleanup and two pinned tables. No vault was opened and no transaction was signed or broadcast.

The public RPC demo rate-limited burst reads. Local SOLANA_RPC_MIN_INTERVAL_MS=1100 spaces Solana requests without retry or rotation; EVM calls are unaffected. Server credential-bearing redirects are rejected. Later approval simulations from this demo returned BlockhashNotFound despite initial simulation success. Approval was withheld, with no unsigned reservation created. The UI now names this failure and requests a fresh preparation or a consistent RPC provider. A dedicated RPC and user-controlled funded round-trip QA remain release requirements.

## Imported finalized trade ledger and exact cashflow totals, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress. Imported Solana projects now expose a finalized trade ledger independently of approval recovery. The owner-authenticated receipts endpoint reads saved verified mainnet receipts across wallet/project revisions and archives. It returns 50-row pages ordered by recorded timestamp and receipt ID, rejects unknown or foreign-project/owner cursors, and validates exact retained mint/decimals, signatures, packet fingerprints, raw token units, settlement, fee/rent/native changes and receipt provenance. Compact allowlisted documents omit transaction packets and credentials. Complete project totals are computed with BigInt from all saved receipts, up to the existing 4000-preparation project bound. Active finalized buys connect to the existing exact acquired-amount/wallet sell review; archived receipts stay readable without the action. CSV export reads the entire current project ledger once rather than exporting only the loaded page. It retains exact raw units and readable decimal strings.

These are recorded trade cashflows, not realized profit, current wallet holdings, complete transaction history or lot attribution. External transfers, other wallet operations and failed-transaction fees are excluded. SOL settlement includes the receipt's observed wrapped-SOL changes and excludes separately recorded network fees/rent. No inferred current balance or market-valued PnL is presented.

Classification: progress. Migration 0049 retains immutable owner-scoped withdrawal contexts, state transitions and events. It bridges the existing warmup/imported wallet locks and permanent public signature markers into a shared execution ledger. Preparation, signing claim and submission atomically recheck the active owned wallet revision and relevant reservation. Signed submissions are claimed before one provider send, with no automatic resend or replacement after an unknown outcome. Recovery checks the exact Ed25519 packet and finalized native balance/fee deltas, works after expiry or archival and releases the wallet only after verified finalization/failure. Event persistence failure rolls back state and prevents sending.

If a wallet returns a signature but registration times out, the browser retains only its public signature, wallet and packet fingerprint in tab storage. It independently verifies and reconstructs the original signed packet on recovery, then retries registration without another signature or broadcast. Explicit SDK rejection or owner-verified absence of a signature can release a signing reservation. A retained returned signature offers registration recovery instead of that release control.

## Standard SPL token withdrawal workflow, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress. The wallet withdrawal panel now supports ordinary standard SPL mints beyond the funding quote-asset whitelist. A holdings row can select the exact wallet, mint and decimals for withdrawal review without reading providers, preparing, signing or sending automatically. Amount and recipient remain explicit user input. Manual mint/precision entry is also available. Archived, stale, frozen, zero or extension-bearing observations cannot initiate the holdings handoff. Preparation independently verifies current finalized mint initialization/decimals, ordinary source wallet, derived associated token accounts, transferable account state, exact uint64 source coverage, destination overflow/rent, SOL fee/rent/reserve coverage, coherent provider slots, simulation and the original live blockhash.

A client-safe canonical message builder is shared with the existing SPL funding preparation. Exact checked transfer bytes and optional idempotent associated-account creation are reconstructed before signing and recovery. Original native SOL documents remain valid. The same immutable withdrawal storage, wallet revision claims, shared wallet locks, permanent public signature markers, user-approved vault/legacy external signing, public-signature retry, separate single send and archived recovery serve both asset types. Token signatures do not trigger automatic sending. Fresh submission checks reject changed precision, ownership, transferable state, coverage, fees or blockhash. Finalized recovery requires the exact signed packet, mint/owner/program/decimals for both token balances, exact source debit/recipient credit and native fee/ATA-rent settlement across every static key. Historic token records display exact mint/precision, amount, finalized slot, fee and actual recipient account rent. The history API independently validates all retained reviews and receipts before returning a page.

Current token scope is standard SPL transfers from the source associated token account. Wrapped SOL, custom source accounts, Token-2022 extensions and EVM transfer adapters remain unfinished. Token-2022 and wrapped SOL are rejected explicitly rather than treated as standard token transfers. Existing native SOL withdrawal and imported Buy/Sell semantics are preserved.

Verification: 44 unique focused tests across eight files passed over multiple runs. The full withdrawal D1 suite covered standard SPL preparation, signature registration, one send, archive/revision recovery and exact finalized token units/ATA rent, plus withholding submission after changed fresh mint precision while retaining its registered signature. Token tests covered missing/existing destination accounts, real Ed25519 signatures, modified asset/precision/accounts/packet, frozen/unsupported token accounts, insufficient units, changed fees/blockhash, forged token receipt identities/units and incorrect native settlement. A 9007199254740993-unit transfer verified precision beyond Number safe integers. Original native withdrawal, legacy external signer, cache recovery, imported signing/activity/receipt and SPL funding tests passed. ## Token-2022 withdrawal adapters, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress. The existing owned withdrawal pipeline now detects standard SPL or Token-2022 mint ownership independently. Canonical associated-account derivation, idempotent account creation and checked transfer instructions use the exact mint program. Supported Token-2022 mint extensions are MetadataPointer, TokenMetadata and TransferFeeConfig; supported token-account extensions are ImmutableOwner and TransferFeeAmount. Unknown, duplicate, malformed, noncanonical or unsupported extension layouts are rejected. Transfer hooks, confidential transfers, non-transferable tokens, interest/scaled units, custom source accounts and wrapped SOL token transfers need separate adapters. Metadata-only and base Token-2022 mints are supported.

Transfer-fee preparation reads the finalized epoch, selects the applicable schedule, calculates ceiling/capped fees with bigint, pins both fee schedules and authorities, and includes the exact expected token fee in TransferCheckedWithFee. Zero-net transfers are rejected. Approval displays gross token debit, withheld fee, recipient net units, epoch/rate/cap, separate SOL fee/rent limits and retained reserve. Fresh submission rechecks the original packet, extension names, epoch and fee configuration. Harvested mint fee balances are excluded from the fee-policy fingerprint. New recipient account rent uses the program's extension-aware size (170 bytes for ordinary Token-2022 ATAs, 182 with transfer fees); existing accounts use their own actual lengths. Finalized receipts verify exact signed bytes, sender gross debit, recipient net credit and all native fee/rent deltas. Retained receipt history displays withheld fee and net received units across archival. Fee amounts are withheld tokens, not a SOL fee or a payment to a third-party wallet.

The receipt review also corrected an existing standard SPL recovery mismatch: the precision-preserving RPC JSON reader returns integer account indices and decimals as strings. The receipt decoder now validates canonical integer evidence in either safe fixture-number or RPC-string representation. Successful standard SPL and Token-2022 receipt tests pass through the actual RPC JSON parser; the Token-2022 API recovery test does so too.

Verification: 54 unique focused tests passed across eight files, including real Ed25519 vault packets, legacy sign-only returned-signature retention, exact token signature-cache restoration, extension/rate/epoch rejection, integer ceiling/cap, metadata extensions, correct account rent sizes, the real D1 prepare/sign/register/single-send/finalize path and archived recovery. The existing imported-signing suite initially timed out during a concurrent build and subsequent tests were affected by the unfinished test; its isolated rerun with a 30-second per-test bound passed all 13 tests. The final withdrawal/regression/fixture run passed 41 tests. TypeScript, focused lint, formatting and production build passed; existing chunk-size, static-route-classification and runtime-deprecation build notices remain. ## Linked warmup lot performance, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress. The previous connection-check turn verified existing Helius configuration without changing product functionality. This turn implements recorded trade performance for warmup buys and their explicitly linked finalized sells. The owner-scoped cycle-progress API reads the complete bounded receipt history in the same D1 batch as the plan and computes results within the exact current-revision or explicitly selected earlier-revision scope. Archived plans remain reviewable. No database migration is needed.

Buy cost is native swap settlement spent plus the successful buy network fee. Linked net sell proceeds are native swap settlement received less successful sell network fees. Cumulative sold units receive a proportional integer allocation of their source buy cost. Full exits consume all rounding residue; the remaining cost belongs to open units. Account rent is displayed separately rather than expensed as a swap fee. Native settlement already accounts for wrapped SOL movements, so it is not reconstructed from an incomplete wallet SOL delta. Tokens of different mints are not combined. No market prices or inferred FIFO allocation are used.

Performance validates the complete history, including excluded lots, before returning totals. Missing economics, duplicate transaction signatures or sells, orphaned links, wallet/mint/precision/program mismatches, sells not following buys, oversold or inconsistent remaining units and receipt capacity overflow fail closed. The view exposes scoped totals and a read-only source lot table with 50-row pagination. Unsold and empty scopes do not display realized gains. It explicitly excludes failed transaction fees, external trades/transfers and pending transactions; it does not claim total account return, tax basis or capital readiness.

Verification: 15 focused tests across accounting, cycle progress, real ephemeral D1 API queries and rendered component output passed. TypeScript, focused lint and the final production build passed. Chrome verified the actual archived QA plan's truthful empty performance state, then an isolated synthetic D1 response verified explicit earlier-revision selection, partial sold/remaining units, exact realized cost, network fees, separate rent and source lot details. The fixture displayed 33 lamports allocated buy cost, 49 lamports net sell proceeds, 16 lamports realized result and 68 lamports remaining cost. Synthetic screenshot: bundle-warmup-performance-qa.png. Actual preview screenshot: bundle-warmup-performance-empty.png. Synthetic data was not inserted into the workspace database. The isolated fixture blocks providers, signing, submission, funding and writes.

## Imported recorded-FIFO performance, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress. The preceding turn completed exact linked warmup lot performance. This turn extends imported project trade history with an explicitly selected recorded-FIFO accounting estimate. Imported sells currently do not retain exact acquisition-lot links, so the UI labels the allocation as a model and starts with its checkbox unchecked. It does not claim exact actual-token attribution or total account return. No schema migration is needed.

The owned finalized receipt API computes performance from its entire bounded 4000-receipt history, independently of the displayed 50-receipt page, across project/wallet revisions and archives. It validates complete unique finalized trade evidence, groups the fixed mint by wallet and orders each wallet by finalized slot. Each sell consumes earlier recorded buys only. Source buy cost includes successful buy network fees; net sell proceeds deduct successful sell network fees. Cumulative proportional integer allocation preserves all basis rounding residue on full exits. Account rent remains separate. Other wallets and later buys never supply an acquisition basis.

Unmatched sold units retain unknown acquisition basis; their proceeds are not treated as gains. Same-slot receipt collisions withhold the entire affected wallet's ordering, realized estimate and open cost, rather than guessing order from a receipt timestamp or ID. Aggregate realized estimates are unavailable if acquisition basis or order is incomplete, or if there are no saved sells. The coverage table retains individually covered wallet estimates and separates unknown-basis units from unresolved-order units. Open recorded cost describes the model's unconsumed app buys, not market value or actual wallet holdings. External trades, holdings, transfers, pending executions and failed transaction fees remain excluded. Exact durable imported buy-lot attribution and complete account performance remain unfinished.

The client validates method, owner-project/mint context, aggregate and wallet counts, amount bounds, sum identities, sold-unit coverage, nullable incomplete results and open cost before rendering. The new accounting view is opt-in, read-only and paginates wallet results in pages of 50. Existing finalized trade filters continue to apply to loaded receipt records; performance uses all saved receipts. Existing raw CSV exports retain the full receipt history for independent reconstruction.

D1 cases cover archived projects, owner isolation, complete results beyond a receipt page, cursor ownership, retained raw CSV export, more than 50 performance wallets and damaged-evidence rejection. ## Imported swap finalized failure evidence, 11 October 2026

## Account dashboard recorded transaction costs, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress. The dashboard now offers an explicit read-only account-cost review. `/api/account-costs` takes one owner-scoped D1 batch snapshot across imported receipts, linked warmup buy/sell lots, saved imported/warmup signing intents and withdrawals. Archived projects and earlier revisions remain included. It reuses finalized imported receipt, linked warmup lot, swap failure and complete native/token withdrawal validators before adding network fees. Exact integer lamports are added separately for successful and failed transactions. Duplicate fee signatures, damaged evidence, oversized histories or failed reads withhold totals. Each query is bounded to 4,001 rows, and the summary rejects more than 4,000 fee receipts. No display-page truncation is used for totals.

Signed/submitted transactions, old failed executions without retained fee proof and finalized swap intents without a saved receipt are counted separately. Missing costs are not assumed zero. The client validates the returned category sums and receipt counts, clears stale totals before refresh and aborts reads on unmount. Mainnet Solana fees from the three checked workflows are the scope. Transfer principal, rent, token transfer fees, funding executions, launches and external transactions are excluded. This is a cost view toward account performance, not proof of full account return or portfolio value. Exact imported buy-lot links, external activity and holding valuation remain required for the full goal.

## Multi-acquisition accounting model, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress. Current authoritative state was inspected before continuing. The prior partial multi-source edit broadened saved metadata types without completing their consumers and produced ten TypeScript errors. Those errors are resolved. A versioned basket can capture two to eight distinct retained buys from the same project, wallet, mint, decimals and accounting revision. Canonical positive u64 allocations must sum exactly to the sell input and captured source evidence must be ordered uniquely by receipt ID. Every source retains its signature, preparation hash, original document hash, acquired/previously assigned units and incremental fee-inclusive cost. Request order is canonicalized; damaged captured ordering is rejected. Legacy single-source records remain valid.

Basket capture loads one complete bounded owner/project snapshot and retains every original source document together. A separate freshness helper recaptures all sources, rejecting changed evidence, capacity or cost. Its explicit unrelated-ledger option ignores only root and leaf accounting revision differences and returns the actual fresh revision for a future compare-and-swap write. It does not modify the approval capture or ignore a changed source. No basket capture is connected to provider work or signing yet.

Linked-result accounting now permits several distinct source links for one sale only when their complete group equals the exact finalized sale input. It rejects duplicate sale/source pairs, incomplete groups, malformed or excess units, altered evidence, wrong-wallet sources, same/later-slot buys and aggregate source overconsumption. One sale is counted once. Its net proceeds after the entire sell fee are apportioned cumulatively in canonical buy-receipt order with exact BigInt arithmetic, preserving all positive or negative rounding residue. Per-source buy costs remain incremental proportional costs and retain the final residue. Rent and failed fees remain separate. Existing manual assignment and single-source execution are unchanged.

Saved preparation, approval, signing-event and shared review types/parsers understand basket metadata; the readonly acquisition review and account Activity detail enumerate each source and total basis. The executable server boundary explicitly rejects baskets until atomic allocation storage exists. The preparation API rejects buyAllocations with HTTP 409 before balances, provider calls or persistence, so a requested basket cannot silently become unlinked inventory. These are implementation prerequisites, not operational multi-source sales. Migration 0054 still enforces one source per sale. Migration/event capacity, atomic all-source reservation/finalization, basket picker and complete approval/receipt recovery remain the next implementation work.

## Multi-buy sale selection, approval and atomic settlement, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress. The preceding model increment left executable baskets disconnected. This increment connects multiple sources through the actual imported sale flow. The picker reads complete owned acquisition history, explicitly switches to multiple-source selection, adds/removes up to eight same-wallet buys, edits exact decimal allocations and shows their remaining capacities and combined fee-inclusive basis. Use allocated total updates the parent trade amount and clears prior quotes/preparations. Duplicate additions and more than eight sources are prevented. A single source, invalid amount, over-capacity source, unread/damaged history or total differing from the sell input disables linked preparation. Changing sources, units, receipt hashes, wallet/project revision or accounting snapshot remounts preparation and aborts old work. Returning to one source preserves a visible selected acquisition; unlinked inventory remains an explicit choice.

The owned preparation API accepts a mutually exclusive buyAllocations vector for Sell only, with a bounded 2048-character request and canonical positive u64 units summing to the input. It captures all original buys from one snapshot before providers, rechecks them after simulation and binds every original document plus accounting revision into the saved preparation write. Approval reservation checks every original document and revision again after independent signing review. The client requires the exact returned source vector and total before passing captured metadata to the explicit signing acknowledgement. Legacy single-source and unlinked trades retain their existing behavior.

Migration 0055 changes link uniqueness to owner/sale/buy and admits partial source rows only for the matching registered approval, packet and finalized receipt. All captured buys are immutable while the approval is pending/signed/submitted. Approval triggers check root context, distinct sources and every matching buy. A recursive decimal-column sum verifies the full input without casting a whole u64 into SQLite's signed integer range. The first combined trigger exceeded D1's expression depth; root, source and exact-total checks were split into independent triggers, retaining the same requirements. Finalization requires the complete captured source set. The server commits one revision/nonce compare-and-swap, the finalized receipt, all source links, terminal intent/event and reservation release in one D1 batch. A failure at a later link, event or nonce guard rolls back all preceding writes. Cancellation and finalized failure consume no sources. Archived receipt recovery remains idempotent and never resends.

The signing-event table is rebuilt transactionally with a 16384-character metadata capacity, preserving original event bytes, sequence IDs and optional failure evidence. New approval/transition/failure-recovery events retain the complete basket. Preparation and signing-intent document limits remain 8192 and are enforced; oversized captures are rejected before reservation. The link reader has a separately derived 32000-row bound for up to eight links per receipt within the existing 4000-receipt complete-history bound. Fifty-row link pagination remains. This is a bounded reader allowance, not a production load-test result or unlimited history claim. Manual post-finalized assignment still assigns an entire unlinked sale to one buy and does not treat an existing split as an idempotent single-source assignment.

## Conditional level reservation and fill lifecycle, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress. Migration 0057 adds per-order/level approval reservations and immutable finalized fills. It was installed only in the existing local development D1 database. A read confirmed both tables, all 20 lifecycle triggers, zero reservations and zero fills. No real conditional approvals or orders were created for verification.

The SQL lifecycle atomically binds a reservation to retained conditional preparation metadata, immutable order terms, exact units, wallet/project revisions and an unexpired sampled condition. A reserved or filled level cannot be prepared again. Order cancellation is withheld while an approval holds a level. Duplicate approvals, including INSERT OR IGNORE attempts against an already-used preparation, cannot leak shared wallet locks. Cancellation and confirmed failure release reservations without consuming a level. A finalized transition requires matching retained finalized receipt evidence and consumes the level once. Receipt, fill, state, shared-wallet release and signing-event persistence roll back together on failure. Previously signed work can finalize across wallet/project archival using its captured immutable context. Approved preparations, intents, fills and linked receipts retain their history.

Verification used actual migration SQL in isolated Miniflare D1. Seventy unique tests across seven files passed: the broad regression run passed 69, including all 38 existing signing tests, and the updated seven-test lifecycle suite added archival recovery coverage. TypeScript, focused lint, formatting and all five production build stages passed. Existing build size/deprecation notices remain. All 12 generated client JavaScript files contained zero configured Jupiter/Helius credential matches. The new SQL fixtures use synthetic signatures and receipts; they prove storage transitions and rollback, not cryptographic signing or funded settlement.

## Scheduled session entry eligibility, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress. The preceding increment added captured session timelines. Saved Buy/Sell sessions now support a UTC start time and exact integer spacing from 0 to 86400 seconds between independent wallet entries. Entry N becomes eligible at start plus (N-1) times spacing. Version-one immediate sessions retain their original canonical document and fingerprint; scheduled terms use version two. Historical decoding preserves both versions and rejects altered schedules, noncanonical dates, fractional/negative/excessive spacing and format/terms mismatches. New schedules must start within the next year, with a one-minute admission tolerance for immediate staged starts; idempotent existing-record retries remain recoverable after that window.

The API derives each entry's notBefore timestamp from the owned immutable session rather than trusting a submitted due time. Preparation and signing review reject early entries before provider work. The timestamp and complete schedule fingerprint are captured in version-two preparation/signing metadata and displayed in the approval review. The exact input, session identity, entry and timestamp are checked in the actual client preparation handoff; local signing checks the retained amount and due time before signing. SQL write guards apply the database's current time to reservation/persistence/submission writes, so an application clock running ahead cannot permit an early write. Separate registered-signature retention and finalized recovery remain intact.

Migration 0060 preserves and extends the existing exact approval trigger for both capture versions and adds three timing triggers for preparation insert, approval insert and submission transition. They derive UTC due times from retained terms, compare the captured timestamp and reject early or altered captures. A legacy capture cannot strip timing from a scheduled session. Immediate version-one work remains supported. A consistent backup preceded transactional installation of the identical tested SQL in the verified project-local D1 file. Readback confirmed all three timing triggers, the approval trigger and zero local session records. No remote migration occurred.

The production session editor includes immediate/scheduled timing, a local-time date control, spacing input, stored UTC review, per-entry UTC times and a foreground countdown. An entry's preparation control becomes available after its own due time; other entries stay disabled until theirs. The server still makes the final timing decision. The approval card renders scheduled eligibility separately from actual submission time, which depends on the user's individual signature and separate submit action. A date-input event problem discovered in Chrome was fixed with explicit input handling, and missing/invalid local starts show a clear validation message. ## Persistent session pause and resume, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress toward the complete original Proxima goal. The preceding increment added scheduled entry eligibility. This increment connects independent persisted ready/paused controls to the production session API, editor, preparation, approval and submission guards. Each new session starts ready at control revision 1. Pause and resume advance only the control revision, preserving the immutable trade terms. Owner-scoped compare-and-swap updates reject stale revisions; identical retries recover the retained result without adding duplicate events. Cancelled sessions cannot resume.

Preparation and signing review reject paused sessions before provider work and recheck after simulation. The final SQL write guard binds the exact control revision, so pausing and resuming between a final read and persistence still invalidates the old review. Database triggers independently block preparation insert, approval insert and submission transitions while paused. Pause clears the editor's preparation panel and disables entry preparation. Existing signatures remain registerable and recoverable, shared reservations remain retained, and already submitted transactions can still settle. Pause does not grant signing authority or revoke a signature.

Pause/resume transitions capture the complete session terms and independent control state/revision in the immutable timeline. GET requires exactly one valid owned control per saved session. Missing, duplicate, foreign or malformed control data fails closed. The project timeline and account Activity render the captured control revision independently of current terms and wallet state.

## Durable scheduled unsigned-preparation jobs, 11 October 2026

Classification: progress toward the complete original Proxima goal. The preceding increment connected direct entry approval recovery. This increment adds durable exact-entry unsigned-preparation jobs, a foreground one-job dispatch action and a standalone scheduled worker. The worker is disabled and not deployed. Each job is an explicit owner's queue action for one retained session entry; saving a session alone does not queue work or authorize a trade.

The ordinary preparation endpoint delegates to prepareOwnedImportedSwap after authentication. The shared service retains all existing project/wallet, input balance, condition/acquisition/session, route/simulation, expiry, provider pacing and atomic persistence checks. The internal worker derives the wallet, amount, project revision and session request from retained owned terms. It never accepts an external owner selector and has no public execution endpoint or signing/submission import. The owner-authenticated job route queues and lists scoped jobs, processes at most one due owned-session job, and separately restores an exact prepared packet. Cross-origin mutations, malformed scopes, duplicate live entries, stale/paused/cancelled context and foreign reads are rejected.

Claims use database time, exact ready control revisions, a 45-second lease and a unique token. One live worker job per owner is allowed. Scheduled dispatch processes at most two jobs per tick. Rate-limit and service errors retry after 30 seconds, up to three attempts; invalid context or unsupported/insufficient balances are terminal. Expired leases requeue after 30 seconds and reject late completions. Parent cancellation/archival cancels queued work. Paused jobs wait. Result persistence requires the same retained control revision and an exact owned preparation-summary/packet binding. Terminal jobs and captured transition events are retained and immutable; event failure rolls back the associated job write.

The job stores a bounded public unsigned payload separately from the ordinary preparation history summary. No key or credential is stored. Packet reads require the owned project/session/job, current owned revisions, active ready due entry with no retained approval, and unexpired quote/balance. Expired packets return 410; fresh queueing creates a new retained attempt. Restoration reports the remaining 15-second wallet-read gate. The actual ImportedSwapPreparation consumes the job packet through its existing wallet/amount/mint/simulation/history/session checks, full instruction/route review and separate eligibility/signature/submission flow. No automatic signing, sending or receipt recovery is added.

Migration 0062 adds two tables, four named indexes including the partial unique live-entry index, and nine lifecycle/admission/event triggers. The schema and journal include it. A consistent SQLite backup preceded transactional installation in the verified project-local D1 database. Readback confirmed both tables, nine triggers and zero jobs/events. No real job or event was created in the application's local database; API tests use isolated Miniflare databases. No remote migration occurred.

### Saved wallet-alert transaction evidence, 11 October 2026

Added authenticated /api/imported-projects/supply-movements GET and POST plus migration 0064_supply_movement_evidence.sql and matching Drizzle schema. Each inspection belongs to an owned saved alert, its exact mint and wallet, and the interval between that wallet's two saved observation slots. Baselines and unrelated wallets are rejected before RPC. Finalized wallet-address signature pages use the existing validated reader, and matching transactions use the existing token balance decoder. Provider anchors must cover the newer observation; mismatched signatures, wallet, network, slot or token precision reject the inspection. Each request examines at most 25 signature entries and fetches at most five transactions. Continuation starts after the last inspected signature and is accepted only if retained for the same owner/project/alert/wallet. Failed signatures and unavailable token decodes remain explicit. Shared supply read pacing is 15 seconds.

Saved evidence is immutable and retained. Publication repeats current project revision and saved observation identity, so a project revision race cannot append stale evidence. GET revalidates stored interval identity and token arithmetic without RPC and returns the latest 20 retained inspection records for that wallet alert. The collapsed Transaction evidence panel lets users inspect, load saved evidence, follow retained older signature pages and open a public Solscan transaction link. Signed decimal deltas use the existing signed formatter. No provider fetch happens merely by rendering an alert. Unmount cancels pending client requests, and project revision changes remount the inspector.

Coverage is the wallet address signature index, not every owned token-account signature. Token-account-only transfers may be absent, and multiple movements can offset between saved balance reads. Reaching an address signature boundary does not prove complete transaction coverage or explain the full observed delta. The UI distinguishes net observation alerts from decoded transactions, discloses this limit, and does not assign buy/sell/counterparty labels. The current page's unavailable/failed counts are shown separately from decoded transactions collected across loaded inspection records.

Verification: 54 tests in eight focused files passed, including five new isolated-D1 API cases and ten new movement-reader cases alongside existing history, signature, transaction, partition and chart checks. TypeScript, focused lint/formatting and all five build stages passed. Chrome synthetic-transport QA verified inspection, the -0.01 token change, transaction link and saved-evidence reload after full page reload. The API tests verify actual isolated-D1 retention independently; the browser persistence fixture uses localStorage only in the QA harness. Desktop and mobile screenshots are bundle-wallet-alert-evidence-qa.png and bundle-wallet-alert-evidence-mobile-qa.png in the chat workspace. ### Token-account signature coverage for saved wallet alerts, 11 October 2026

Added current token-account discovery and account-specific signature inspection to the existing saved wallet-alert API and UI. Find token accounts issues an authenticated, paced POST for an owned saved alert. The provider reader checks mainnet genesis, uses finalized getTokenAccountsByOwner with the alert wallet slot as minContextSlot, and validates owner, mint, SPL/Token-2022 program, executable flag, parsed type, initialized/frozen state, token precision, raw u64 balance and unique public keys. Enumerations larger than 500 are rejected. Zero-balance current accounts are included. Signature pages use the selected account, while transaction decoding remains bound to the original wallet and mint. Matching token changes must include the selected token account in their decoded balance accounts. Persisted evidence records carry the indexed public address and finalized ownership observation slot. The existing immutable JSON table supports these fields without a schema migration; historical wallet-index records remain compatible. Continuation cursors are additionally bound to the indexed address, preventing a retained wallet/account cursor from being replayed against another signature index. GET remains provider-free and returns saved account indexes with the latest 20 inspection records.

The alert inspector lets users choose Wallet address or discovered/saved token accounts, inspect each index, and follow that index's continuation. Loaded decoded transactions are deduplicated by signature across indexes; a mint-wide wallet change is displayed once. Discovery is manual and shares the existing 15-second read gate. No automatic RPC fanout occurs on opening an alert. Selected-index boundary coverage and unavailable decode counts are shown explicitly. Long index addresses wrap on mobile. Restoring saved evidence restores the available historical index options, while discovery itself describes current accounts only.

## 2026-10-12: user-approved Jito bundle flow

Added a local Jito bundle flow under each saved imported Solana trade session. Up to four current wallet swaps are prepared together, reviewed as one immutable ordered manifest, individually signed with explicit approval, and submitted with a separately approved tip as a maximum five-transaction bundle. Stored signature verification, owner/project/wallet/session guards, transactional membership/reservations and one-shot transport prevent accidental individual submission or automatic duplicate/fallback sends. Unknown responses remain recoverable; finalization requires exact same-slot chain outcomes and saved swap receipts. Entirely unsigned bundles can be cancelled; signed expiry recovery is evidence-driven.
